Silver is complete, and running real data through it found that the pipeline was silently deleting real events.** Legacy events carry no native id, so Task 4 hashes `(created_at, actor_login, repo_id, event_type)` to make one — a field set the plan describes as "the minimal set that distinguishes two genuinely different events". Measured against a real legacy hour: **two colliding pairs in 2,000 events, and neither pair was a duplicate.** One actor pushed two different commit ranges to one repo inside the same second; another opened issues #13232 and #13229 inside the same second. Dedup deleted one of each — roughly **1 in 1,000 legacy events, silently**. Phase 0's measured duplicate ratio of 1 in 6.0M could not have caught it: that sample was modern data, which carries native ids and never reaches this hash. `event_url` is non-null on all 2,000 sampled and takes the same hour to **zero** collisions; it is stable content (a canonical URL naming the specific commit range or issue) rather than a serialization artifact, which is the line that keeps the rest of the payload out of the key. The legacy fixture went from 1,995 clean to 1,997. **Task 4 and Task 5 could not be composed at all.** `normalize_events` did not carry `ingested_at`, which is exactly the column `deduplicate` orders on — so the Silver order the plan declares (normalize, then dedup) raised `UNRESOLVED_COLUMN` the first time anything tried it. Both tasks were green, and so was CI, because nothing had wired them together; the same omission also made `created_at_not_future` — a rule declared back in Task 1 — unrunnable, since it compares against `ingested_at`. That is the **third consecutive task with an interface gap, and the first between two already-committed modules** rather than between a plan's prose and its code. `ingested_at` is now part of the Silver contract, and a new test applies each declared rule on its own so a failure names the rule instead of failing the pipeline with one unresolved column. **The plan lists `silver.py` under "Files: Create" and never specifies it** — `run_silver` was written from the design's declared Silver order. It takes `ingested_at` as a required argument rather than reading the clock, per Task 2's finding: a clock read would make a replay of the same file produce different rows *and still pass a count-based idempotency check*. Era dispatch lives at the read boundary and is read from the parsed schema rather than the filename's date. Legacy `repo_name` is reconstructed as `owner/name` to match modern's already-qualified `owner/repo` — 1,997 of 2,000 legacy records carry both — because leaving it unqualified would make the column mean two different things either side of 2015 and Phase 2's SCD2 would read the era boundary as a mass rename of every repo that survived it; joined with `concat`, which propagates nulls, not `concat_ws`, which would emit a bare unqualified name. **Four further plan defects:** (1) the integration test opens `2025-08-13-14.jsonl.gz`, which does not exist — the fixture is `modern-2025-08-13-14.jsonl.gz`, and `conftest.py` already had session fixtures resolving both eras; (2) its test parameters are unannotated, the fourth task in a row `mypy --strict`'s `disallow_incomplete_defs` has rejected planned test code; (3) `-> "F.Column"` is not a real path — `Column` comes from `pyspark.sql`; (4) conservation is asserted as `clean + quarantined <= raw`, which dropping every row satisfies — rewritten as an equation with the removed duplicates named and added back, and the `integration` marker registered. **Task 1's open question settled:** `event_type_known` only asserted `event_type IS NOT NULL`, and that name would have appeared in quarantine analysis as though a membership check had run; renamed to `event_type_present`. Five mutations run, each killed by exactly one test. |
| 2026-09-01 | Phase 1 Task 7 — calibration on Azure | Databricks job run `404509887846902`, `westus3`; five runs, 0.436 cluster-hours, **$1.03** total; `make check` (130 tests) | **Green, and the number Phase 1 existed to buy is measured: 13.84 GB gz per billed cluster-hour.** One day (2025-08-13), 24/24 hours with no gaps, 3,794,323 rows, 2.012 GB gz — within 4% of Phase 0's independent ~2.1 GB/day estimate. **The plan's script could not have run.** Verified locally before spending anything: `write_bronze` partitions by `event_date`/`event_hour` and the planned script never adds those columns, so it fails with `DELTA_PARTITION_COLUMN_NOT_FOUND` — after paying for cluster startup. It also calls `hours_in_range` with datetimes when it takes dates and yields `(date, hour)` pairs (Task 3's defect, third appearance), and `len()` on that generator. `expected_hours` from Task 3 replaced all of it. **One measurement decision changed the answer materially:** the plan asks for a single wall-clock figure, which cannot separate a slow cluster from a slow network. Timing fetch and Spark separately gives 36.72 GB/hr by Spark time, 19.50 by wall clock, and **13.84 per *billed* cluster-hour once setup is counted** — the only one of the three that is what you actually pay for. A single-timer measurement would have understated the bill by a third. **A third of the billed time (174.3 s of 523.5 s) was single-threaded HTTP download**, which is the obvious Phase 2 cost lever and is deliberately not built now. **Four failed runs, each a fact worth recording** rather than a fix guessed at: the job launcher will not read `python_file` from a Unity Catalog volume even with `READ_VOLUME` granted (workspace files work — and the first grant fix *didn't* work, which is what said the component had been misnamed); **public DBFS root is disabled on this workspace**; and `spark.read.json` infers a different schema for different hours of the same day, so hour N's write rejects hour N+1. That last one is a data finding, not a config one, and its fix is a design correction: **Bronze now reads raw text, not parsed JSON** — parsing is a transformation and Bronze never transforms, which is exactly what Task 2's own Bronze tests already assumed with their `raw_json string` schema. Raw text also has one schema for every hour and every era. **The recorded cluster cost was 25% low:** §4.5 had $1.896/hr for "4 × `D4ds_v6`", and live Azure Retail Prices API rates reproduce that exactly at four nodes — but `num_workers: 4` provisions five VMs, four workers and a driver. Corrected to $2.370/hr. **Tier 3 derived and committed with its arithmetic above: the full Q3 2025 quarter, 92 days, $31.71, 17.2% of the credit against a 40% cap.** §4.5 says the rule binds in both directions; it bound upward, from one month to a quarter. Teardown verified: 0 active clusters. |

Install is clean (`dbt-spark` declares `pyspark<5.0.0,>=3.0.0`). Incremental `merge` and SCD2 snapshots both work on local `local[*]` Delta — **but only with a persistent Hive metastore, and the failure mode is silent**: with the default in-memory metastore dbt cannot see the existing relation, `is_incremental()` is false, and both models execute `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE AS SELECT` while reporting `success=True`. Both Phase 1 defects are fixed, and executing the task corrected the plan that described it.** Silver now writes `partitionBy(event_date, event_hour)` with `replaceWhere` scoped to the day, and reads Bronze rather than `spark.read.json(source_path)`. `run_silver` lost its `ingested_at` parameter entirely — Bronze stamped it and Silver reads it back with the row, which removes a class of error rather than documenting it. **The plan's Step 2 was wrong and the fixture disproved it.** It said to derive `event_date`/`event_hour` from `created_at` because "a late-arriving event belongs to the hour it happened in". Measured on the committed legacy fixture: one archive file named hour 14 holds events from 14:05 to 15:01 at `-07:00` — **UTC 21:05 to 22:01, 1,957 events in hour 21 and 43 in hour 22**. Deriving the partition from `created_at` scatters one source file across two partitions and no hour-scoped `replaceWhere` can then replace that file's contribution idempotently. Partition by ingest, reason by event time: `created_at` stays the event-time column every fact and feature is computed from. **The grain is a day, not an hour**, decided for a reason the plan did not state: §12 trap 4 is that duplicate ids occur across hour-file boundaries, so an hour-at-a-time Silver would dedup inside each file, never see the boundary, and quietly undo Phase 1's Task 5 while every unit test stayed green. Day-boundary duplicates remain out of scope, stated rather than accidental. **Parsing uses one explicit union schema for all three eras**, with `actor` the single exception — it is a bare string before 2015 and an object after, which no struct field can express, so it is read with `get_json_object` (measured: `$.actor.login` resolves on 2,000 of 2,000 modern and 0 of 2,000 legacy). Probed before being relied on: 0 whole-struct parse failures on either fixture. **Two measured findings changed the code.** Legacy `IssueCommentEvent` carries no `issue.pull_request` at all — 0 of 194, against 55 of 92 modern — so a structural `IS NOT NULL` would report every legacy issue comment as "not on a PR", a fabricated negative that would silently drop the whole legacy era out of §5.1's label, which already has no `PullRequestReviewEvent` there. `is_pr_comment` is therefore **null in the legacy era**, and Gold can still resolve those by joining `(repo_id, pr_number)` against known PRs, since GitHub numbers issues and PRs from one per-repo sequence. `pr_draft` is likewise null rather than false pre-2015 (149 of 149 modern non-null, 0 of 106 legacy). **One of my own tests was wrong and failing it was the useful part:** the first `test_a_second_hour_does_not_destroy_the_first` wrote the same fixture to both hours and expected 4,000 rows; dedup correctly collapsed them to 2,000. That is trap 4 working, not the defect — so the test now lands disjoint halves the way two real hourly files do, and the duplicate case became its own test, the first to check cross-hour dedup through the *runner* rather than on crafted rows. **Three mutations, each killed:** reverting to `mode("overwrite")` and dropping the `read_bronze` date filter each reddened exactly the two day-scale tests (and correctly not the single-call hour test, where the defect cannot manifest); removing the era-nulling of `is_pr_comment` reddened its two tests — after one of them was corrected, because it had been passing for the wrong reason, asserting on a defaulted null where `parse_events` really emits `false`. The raw-row builder moved into `tests/helpers.py`: it was spelled out in two test modules, and adding a parser column broke whichever one was not being looked at — the same two-places-one-contract failure that cost Phase 1 a day. `ruff` gained `PLR0913` for tests, with the rationale recorded next to the existing `N812` note. |
| 2026-09-01 | Phase 2 Task 2 — dbt scaffold on Delta, and the silent-rebuild regression test | `make check` (ruff, ruff format, mypy --strict on 43 files, 150 tests); two dbt runs in two separate processes against a real Derby metastore; `make dbt` against the shipped project | **Green, 150 tests (was 148), and the defect the spike predicted was reproduced for real before it was fixed.** The scaffold was deliberately built *without* the metastore first, so the test could be watched failing for the right reason: two consecutive `dbt run` invocations produced `['CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE AS SELECT', 'CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE AS SELECT']` in the Delta commit log — dbt reporting `success=True` both times while silently rebuilding the model. The defect is a property of the *target*, not of any model, so the test drives a canary model under `tests/fixtures/dbt_canary/` against the profile this repo actually ships; no dead model is left in `dbt/models/`. (2) Task 2's file list omits an entry point, but `make dbt` cannot exist without one — dbt-spark's session method calls `SparkSession.builder.enableHiveSupport().getOrCreate()`, which *reuses* the live session and cannot retrofit Delta or a metastore onto it, so the session must be built first. `src/almanac/gold/runner.py` (listed in the phase's file table, unassigned to a task) lands here as the minimal version. (3) The `databricks` target uses dbt-spark's `http` method rather than a second adapter: `SparkConnectionMethod` was inspected live and carries `HTTP`/`THRIFT`/`ODBC`/`SESSION`, so one adapter covers both targets. Its `env_var` defaults are empty on purpose — dbt renders the whole profiles file before selecting a target, so a bare `env_var` would make the local target unusable on any machine that has never touched Databricks. Versions were re-checked live rather than carried from the plan and matched it exactly (dbt-core 1.12.3, dbt-spark 1.11.0). **`dbt build` warns `Nothing to do` and flags `models.almanac.gold` as an unused config path** — correct at this point, since Gold has no models until Task 4, and stated here so it is not mistaken later for a regression. |

| 2026-09-02 | Phase 2 Task 4 — `fact_pull_request`, event-native accumulating snapshot | `make check` (ruff, ruff format, mypy --strict, 158 tests); `make dbt` on the committed fixtures; a mutation dropping the `least()` fold; a lifecycle across separate dbt processes; real-fixture counts probed | **Green, 158 tests (was 151).** One row per `(repo_id, pr_number)`; every column derived from an event-level field — the event's own `created_at` / `actor_login` / `event_type` / `event_action` — never `payload.pull_request.*`. §4.3a is enforced mechanically by a grep guard over the **comment-stripped** SQL (`merged` / `draft` are the documented exception, carried from Silver's already-parsed `pr_merged` / `pr_draft`, era-bound and null-safe). Incremental `merge` on `(repo_id, pr_number)`; on a rerun the model folds the new batch against the stored row with `least()` / `greatest()` over a `full outer join {{ this }}`, so a later batch carrying no `opened` event cannot overwrite a real `opened_at` with null — **mutation-confirmed**: replacing `least(batch.closed_at, prior.closed_at)` with a plain `batch.closed_at` reddens exactly `test_out_of_order_open_after_close_keeps_both_timestamps` and nothing else. `last_ingested_at` is the per-row high-water mark driving the `ingested_at > max(...)` incremental filter: `ingested_at` is processing time and advances monotonically across a resumable backfill, so a strict `>` neither re-reads nor skips. `is_censored = closed_at is null`, flagged not fabricated (§5.1); Task 5 excludes these from the label. **Two test bugs, both caught by watching the run fail rather than by re-reading:** (1) the integration tests compared *collected* datetimes — the naive-datetime trap Phase 1 Task 2 already paid for (09:00 UTC read back as 05:00 on a UTC-4 driver); rewritten to project every timestamp column through `unix_timestamp(...)` and compare epochs, the rule the rest of the repo follows. (2) the §4.3a grep guard matched `payload.pull_request` **in the model's own docstring**, which names it precisely to say it is *not* read; the guard now strips `--` comments first. **Measured on the committed fixtures:** 319 distinct PRs (149 modern PR-event PRs + legacy PR-event PRs + 68 review-only); `first_review_at` populated on exactly the 68 modern review PRs and **0 legacy** — that era has no `PullRequestReviewEvent` (§4.3a's measured fact, and §5.1's second argument for the broadened label); `draft` non-null on exactly the 80 modern opened PRs; censoring rate 63.3% on this mid-stream slice. `assert_fact_pull_request_grain.sql` runs the one-row-per-PR invariant on every `build`, and `test_second_build_merges_rather_than_rebuilding` now asserts the `MERGE`-on-rerun from the Delta commit log for this model directly, covering that Phase 2 exit-gate item. |
| 2026-09-02 | Phase 2 Task 5 — the §5.1 label, `agg_repo_daily`, and the Silver + incremental-strategy changes they forced | `make check` (ruff, ruff format, mypy --strict, 166 tests); `make dbt` on the committed fixtures (19 dbt nodes); two mutations; a Java-`rlike`-vs-Python-`re` agreement check on 21 real logins | **Green, 166 tests (was 158).** The label is `time_to_first_response_seconds` on `fact_pull_request`: `first_response_at − opened_at`, where `first_response_at` is the earliest `PullRequestReviewEvent` / `PullRequestReviewCommentEvent` / PR `IssueCommentEvent` by an actor **other than the PR author**, and never before `opened_at` (§2). Author exclusion is **mutation-confirmed** — deleting the `actor_login <> author_login` filter reddens `test_label_counts_only_non_author_human_responses` because the author's own comment then counts. `label_exclusion` states *why* a PR is out of the trainable population — `author_unobserved` (no `opened` event → the exclusion itself cannot be evaluated, §5.1's stated-exclusion rule), `draft` (null-safe: a legacy null `draft` is unknown, not "not a draft", so a legacy PR still gets a label), `open_unobserved`, `right_censored` (open, no response yet), `closed_no_response` (closed, none ever) — and is null **iff** the label is non-null, which `assert_label_exclusion_accounts_for_every_row.sql` enforces on every build so no row can carry a null label with no stated reason. **`fact_pull_request`'s incremental strategy was rebuilt, not just extended.** Task 4's `least()` scalar fold cannot do author exclusion when a response lands in a batch *before* the `opened` event — the per-actor response detail it needs is not in the row. Replaced with **recompute-touched**: any PR with a new event this batch is rebuilt in full from a new `int_pr_events` view (the one place "which event types feed a PR" is decided, including the legacy `IssueCommentEvent` → known-PR resolution, since `is_pr_comment` is null pre-2015); untouched rows pass through from `{{ this }}`. Out-of-order arrival is now correct by construction and the `least()` fold is gone — mutation-confirmed: reverting `scope` to the watermark slice instead of the full `int_pr_events` join reddens `test_out_of_order_open_after_close_keeps_both_timestamps`. **`author_is_bot` is a first-class column** via a new `almanac_is_bot` dbt macro that must stay byte-identical to `measure.BOT_REGEX` (the anchored heuristic a bare `ci$` clause failed — 869 human surnames). `test_bot_macro.py` asserts the two pattern strings are identical **and** that Spark `rlike` (a Java regex engine) agrees with Python `re` on 21 real logins including `BarisYazici`, `swift-ci`, `VenlyCI`, `cw-circleci` — the engines differ and the string alone does not prove agreement. Bots are flagged and segmented on, never dropped (§5.1). **`agg_repo_daily`**: daily grain per repo on **event time**, `stars_gained` (there are no un-star events, so a running total is not derivable — §12 traps 1–2), `commits_pushed = sum(payload.size)` **never `size(commits)`** (capped at 20 — trap 3), `bot_events`, PR/fork/issue counts. Same recompute-touched incremental strategy; `assert_agg_repo_daily_grain.sql` pins the daily grain and `test_daily_grain_and_out_of_order_events_recompute_not_double_count` proves a late event corrects a prior day rather than adding to a stale total. **Silver gained `push_size` / `push_distinct_size`** — `agg_repo_daily` needs `payload.size` and Silver did not carry it; two nullable columns on the explicit parse schema, `push_distinct_size` null pre-2015 (measured: the legacy `PushEvent` payload is `{shas, size, ref, head}` only). **Measured on the committed fixtures** (one modern + one legacy hour): `fact_pull_request` = 449 PRs, and **every one carries a `label_exclusion`** — a single hour never contains a full open→response→close arc, so `time_to_first_response_seconds` is exercised only by the crafted lifecycle tests, not the fixture (author_unobserved 312, right_censored 129, draft 4, closed_no_response 4; `author_is_bot` 25; 0 label/exclusion mismatches). `agg_repo_daily` = 3,522 repo-days, 192 stars gained, 16,822 commits pushed, 433 bot events. **Still open for Task 9**: §5.1 does not yet state the era-bound label coverage (no `PullRequestReviewEvent` before 2015) or the `closed_no_response` category — deferred there per that task's Step 7. |
| 2026-09-02 | Phase 2 Task 6 — enforced contracts, referential tests, and the proof they fail the build | `make check` (ruff, ruff format, mypy --strict, 169 tests); `make dbt` on the committed fixtures (23 dbt nodes); a copied-project break-it test per mechanism | **Green, 169 tests (was 166).** `fact_pull_request` and `agg_repo_daily` carry **`contract: enforced`** with a `data_type` on every column, paired with `on_schema_change: fail` — a column added or retyped breaks the build rather than drifting in. Proven, not asserted: `test_a_wrong_output_type_fails_the_contract` copies the shipped `dbt/` project to a tmp dir, emits `repo_id` as a string there, and confirms `dbt build` fails with a contract error (the shipped project is never touched). **`relationships`** from `fact_pull_request.repo_id` and `agg_repo_daily.repo_id` to `dim_repo`: `test_a_fact_row_with_no_dim_repo_fails_relationships` drops the repo from the dimension in the copy and confirms the build reddens. Plus `not_null` on every key and non-nullable column, `accepted_values` on `int_pr_events.kind` and `fact_pull_request.label_exclusion`, `not_null` on `dim_repo.repo_id`. **`int_pr_events` is deliberately *not* contract-enforced** — it is an internal view, and `dbt_project.yml`'s blanket `+materialized: incremental` for `gold/` collides with `contract` + `on_schema_change` on a view at parse time (`Invalid value for on_schema_change: ignore`, no model named); the two consumer models the feature platform and BI actually read carry the enforced contracts, which is where enforcement earns its keep. **Two dbt gotchas, both found the slow way.** (1) `contract: enforced` on an incremental model requires `on_schema_change` set to `append_new_columns` or `fail`, and dbt-1.12 reads that pairing from the **schema.yml `config:` block** — the value in the model's `{{ config() }}` is silently ignored for that check (four failed `dbt parse` runs, error never naming a model). (2) Adding the `relationships` tests broke every per-model integration test that runs `dbt build --select <one model>`: the cross-model test's *other* parent isn't built, so it errors `TABLE_OR_VIEW_NOT_FOUND`. `--indirect-selection cautious` fixes the ones where the test is pulled in *indirectly* (via the model), but **not** `--select dim_repo+`, where the `+` selects the test as a graph descendant — `--indirect-selection` cannot exclude a directly-selected node. Dropped the `+`; the snapshot's own `assert_dim_repo_scd2` / `not_null` are still pulled in indirectly. `dbt build` has run on the fixtures in CI since Task 3, so the full contract + relationships suite runs there on every push; no workflow change was needed. |
| 2026-09-02 | Phase 2 Task 7 — the GitHub REST API second source, as a falsifiable gate | `make check` (ruff, ruff format, mypy --strict, 175 tests); the bound computed from measured Phase 0 numbers; §4.5a corrected in the same commit | **Green, 175 tests (was 169), and §4.5a's "zero new Python" claim is falsified — cleanly.** `conf/sources/github_rest.yml` (13 non-comment lines) *describes* the source completely and **nothing in the framework consumes it**: the only config field the Spark path reads is `quality_rules`, and the only fetch path is `archive_url(day, hour)` → one gzip file (`url_template` had been a declared field that nothing read). Onboarding a paginated, authenticated, rate-limited API took **~123 non-comment lines of new Python** — 29 for `AuthConfig` / `RateLimitConfig` / `RestSourceConfig` in `pipeline/source.py` (`SourceConfig` is `extra="forbid"` and its fields do not describe an API; `test_the_file_source_config_rejects_the_rest_config` pins that the shapes do not overlap), ~94 for `extract/rest.py`: `RestSession` + `fetch_pr` (Bearer auth, enrichment extraction), rate-limit backoff that waits **until the response's own `X-RateLimit-Reset`** rather than a fixed guess (`test_fetch_pr_backs_off_when_the_budget_is_exhausted`), and `Link`-header pagination as a generator (`test_list_pr_numbers_follows_the_link_header`). **The honest register statement** (`docs/findings/2026-09-02-second-source.md`, and §4.5a now corrected to match): the framework onboards a *file* source by YAML alone and needs ~120 lines of Python for an API — the claim held for the easy case and broke on the one that matters, which is more credible than an unfalsifiable claim of full generality. **The bound on the enrichment set is measured, not guessed**: from Phase 0's −97% PR-event rate on 2026 data (~200 opened PRs/hr), a 5% repo sample of one week of current data is ~1,680 requests — a third of one 5,000/hr window. **Tests never touch the network** (`httpx.MockTransport` throughout; `RestSession` carries injectable `sleep` / `now`); the client has never made a real request, and the enrichment is a `PrEnrichment` dataclass, not a Delta table joined to Gold — that wiring is Phase 3+, since the model is rich-era-only by design. |

Gold stays out of the per-day loop by design — dbt builds the whole warehouse, not a day of it. **Not done (burn-time):** parallelise-vs-measure the fetch on a real cluster, the two named cost-risk watches, measure-and-publish per layer, teardown verification. |

Fixed with two Unity Catalog volumes, `almanac_dbx.burn.checkpoints` and `almanac_dbx.burn.photon_ab` (the alternative `backfill_checkpoint_dir`'s own comment had already named but not tried) — `/Volumes/...` is FUSE-mounted and pathlib-compatible without the restriction, verified writable by the same `databricks fs cp` probe. **A second, unrelated deploy gap**: `backfill_python_file` / `photon_ab_python_file` / `almanac_wheel` defaulted to `/Workspace/Repos/almanac/...`, which assumes a Databricks Repo linked to this repo's git remote — not configured here. Used `databricks sync` to `/Workspace/Shared/almanac/` instead (checksummed identical to the local wheel after upload) and updated the three defaults to match, deliberately avoiding a `/Workspace/Users/<email>/...` path so a personal email is not what ends up committed. `terraform plan` after both fixes: **no changes** — committed config now matches the live, verified-working state exactly, so a future `apply` cannot silently revert either fix. Azure quota re-checked live rather than assumed from Task 9's record: Ddsv6 48/48 vCPUs available, the job needs 20. **The actual backfill run has not started yet** — this row is setup only. |

Fixed by adding a `source_config_workspace_path` variable, wired into both jobs' `parameters`. (2) **Reading `photon_ab.py`'s own argparse before this could bite the same way at 2x the cost**: `databricks_job.photon_ab`'s task parameters open directly with `--photon`/`--no-photon`, but `main()` dispatches on a required subcommand (`run`/`compare`) — the literal token `"run"` was never in the parameter list, which fails before any flag is even parsed. Caught by reading the script against the terraform that calls it, not by running it; fixed in the same commit as (1), one turn ahead of triggering that job for real. `terraform plan` after the fix: 2 to change (both in-place), applied clean. **Cost of the failed run**: cluster provisioned and one Python process failed within its first minute of Spark being live — a few cents against the $184 credit, not the $31.71 the full run would have cost had this failure mode instead been a silent wrong-answer one. Re-triggering now. |
| 2026-09-02 | Phase 2 burn — second real run, and a defect invisible to every local Spark session | Triggered `databricks_job.backfill` for real (run `347766258130286`); `databricks jobs get-run-output` on the failed task; `make check` (ruff, ruff format, mypy --strict, 202 tests, was 201) | **Got further — fetched a real day of data — then failed on a path that was never actually under DBFS.** `UnsupportedOperationException: Public DBFS root is disabled` on `/local_disk0/almanac-staging/2025-07-01-0.json.gz`, genuine local disk. Root cause: `_land_bronze`'s `spark.read.text(str(result.path))` (and `scripts/calibrate.py`'s identical line) passes a bare, scheme-less path, and Spark resolves a bare path against Hadoop's `fs.defaultFS` — `dbfs:/` on this cluster, `file:///` on every local Spark session this project's tests have ever run, which is exactly why nothing local could have caught it. Fixed with `.as_uri()` on both call sites, forcing the `file://` scheme regardless of the cluster's default. `scripts/build_silver_fixture.py` has the identical pattern at its one call site and was deliberately left alone — it only ever runs via `local_session()`, never on a cluster, so it was never exposed. **New regression test** sets a hostile `fs.defaultFS` (`dbfs:/nonexistent`) on the real `spark` fixture and asserts `process_day` still reads the file; its bronze/silver paths are given their own explicit `file://` scheme, matching their real `abfss://` scheme in production, so only the staging read — bare on every real run too — is exposed to the hostile default, matching production's actual blast radius rather than over-broadly breaking the test's own Delta writes. Full suite re-run standalone after the fix: **202 passed in 1312.17s**. **Both failed real runs, and the two proactive pre-run fixes, written up together**: `docs/findings/2026-09-02-burn-deploy-and-first-run-defects.md` and the interview story bank gist (Story 25) — logged before the third trigger, at the user's explicit instruction, rather than waiting for the saga to close. Re-triggering now. |

Root cause, confirmed by reading the live workspace rather than guessing from the stack trace: this workspace's only Unity Catalog storage credential (`almanac_dbx`) is scoped to the metastore's own managed storage account (`dbstoragecyy2suzhxmuq4`) — what backs the UC volumes defect #1 already fixed — and was never wired to this project's own lake storage account (`azurerm_storage_account.lake`) at all, Unity Catalog or legacy key-based. **`terraform validate` and `plan` structurally could not have caught this**: every prior defect was a wrong value in a resource that existed; this one was an entire resource class — a storage credential — that was never declared, so there was no drift to diff against, only a runtime failure once a cluster actually tried the path. Fixed with new `infra/terraform/unity_catalog.tf`: an `azurerm_databricks_access_connector` (system-assigned managed identity) granted `Storage Blob Data Contributor` on the lake storage account via `azurerm_role_assignment`, feeding a `databricks_storage_credential` (Azure managed identity), with a `databricks_external_location` + `databricks_grants` (`READ_FILES`/`WRITE_FILES`/`CREATE_EXTERNAL_TABLE`, granted to the SINGLE_USER cluster's actual running identity) per medallion container — all four (bronze/silver/gold/features), not just the two currently used, so gold's turn doesn't repeat this same discovery later. This is a real, standing IAM grant on real Azure infrastructure, not an app-code change, so it was held for an explicit go-ahead beyond the burn's original one before `apply` ran — given, then applied clean: **11 added, 0 changed, 0 destroyed**, storage credential validated the role assignment could actually reach the account on first try (no propagation-delay retry needed). All six defects across all three failed real triggers written up together in `docs/findings/2026-09-02-burn-deploy-and-first-run-defects.md`. Re-triggering now (fourth attempt; no code change since the third, so no wheel rebuild needed). |

New `backfill_staging_dir` Terraform variable wired into **both** `databricks_job.backfill` and `databricks_job.photon_ab`'s `--staging-dir` (`photon_ab.py` calls the identical `process_day()` path and its clusters also run 4 workers, so it carried the same latent bug before ever running for real). `photon_ab`'s `--warehouse`/`--metastore` deliberately left on `/local_disk0` — Gold's dbt/Derby path hasn't been exercised on this cluster yet, flagged rather than fixed speculatively. `terraform plan` after: 0 to add, 2 to change, 0 to destroy. Defect #7 of eight now written up in `docs/findings/2026-09-02-burn-deploy-and-first-run-defects.md`. Re-triggering now (fifth attempt). |
| 2026-09-02 | Phase 2 burn — fifth real run, and a read-after-write consistency lag on a brand-new UC volume | Triggered `databricks_job.backfill` for real (run `1111960589912821`); `databricks jobs get-run-output` on the failed task; `databricks fs ls` on the staging volume directly, both immediately after failure and again minutes later; TDD: three new tests in `tests/integration/test_burn.py` injecting the real error shape at `DataFrameReader.text`; `make check` (ruff, ruff format, mypy --strict, 205 tests, was 202); `uv build` + `databricks workspace import --overwrite` to redeploy the wheel | **Cleared defect #7 and failed fast again — 83 seconds, not 45 minutes — for a reason that only exists because #7's fix worked.** `[PATH_NOT_FOUND] Path does not exist: file:/Volumes/almanac_dbx/burn/staging/2025-07-01-0.json.gz. SQLSTATE: 42K03`. Checked the volume directly after the cluster had already terminated: all 24 of the day's hourly files were present, including the one Spark said it couldn't find — same size, same `last_modified`, written in a ~12-second parallel burst. The file was never missing. **The diagnosis recorded here — that it was merely not yet visible — was wrong, and run `156595311273405` in the next row falsified it; the real cause is the `file:` URI scheme.** What follows in this row is what was believed and done at the time: `_land_bronze` processes hours in order starting at 0, so hour 0 is read essentially the instant the parallel fetch burst finishes, with zero elapsed time for the UC volume's FUSE mount to settle; every later hour gets progressively more real wall-clock time before its own read, which is presumably why only hour 0 was ever observed to fail (a freshly created volume may worsen the window; unconfirmed). Fixed with a bounded retry around the read+write for one hour (`almanac.burn.day._land_hour`, new `Settings.max_land_attempts` / `land_retry_seconds`), catching only the `PATH_NOT_FOUND` string — anything else still fails on attempt one, same discipline as `fetch_hour`'s own HTTP retries. Safe to retry the whole read+write, not just the read, because `write_bronze`'s `replaceWhere` is already idempotent per hour. **TDD, not a guessed patch redeployed blind**: three tests monkeypatch `DataFrameReader.text` to raise the exact real error shape, proving retry-past-one-failure, give-up-after-budget, and no-retry-on-an-unrelated-exception, before the fix was trusted with another paid run. `make check` green (205 tests). Wheel rebuilt and re-uploaded via `databricks workspace import --overwrite`, not `databricks sync` — `dist/.gitignore` is `*`, which sync's gitignore-awareness would have silently skipped the one file that needed to move. No Terraform change needed; `almanac_wheel`'s path is unchanged, only its content. Defect #8 of eight written up in `docs/findings/2026-09-02-burn-deploy-and-first-run-defects.md`. Re-triggering now (sixth attempt). |
| 2026-09-02 | Phase 2 burn — seventh run succeeds, and its own timings expose a single-threaded Bronze | Read run `227270110474807`'s per-day checkpoint files from the staging volume; compared per-layer timings across a cold day and two steady-state days; read `_land_bronze` against the gzip splittability property | **The scheme fix held — day 1 completed all 24 hours, 3,748,409 rows, zero quarantined — and the run has processed 65+ days at a stable ~3.2 min/day with no degradation as the Delta tables grow.** Steady state per day: fetch ~13 s (7%), Bronze ~130 s (65%), Silver ~57 s (28%). **The anomaly: Bronze does far less work than Silver and takes 2.3x longer.** Silver parses 3.7 M JSON documents, normalizes three schema eras, shuffles for dedup, runs six quality rules and writes two tables in ~57 s; Bronze reads a file, adds two literal columns and writes, in ~130 s. Diagnosis: `_land_bronze` loops hour by hour, and a `.json.gz` is **non-splittable** — one file is one Spark input partition, one task, one core — so Bronze runs 24 single-core jobs in series while the 4-worker cluster sits largely idle. Silver is fast for the mirror-image reason: it reads Delta, which fans out across its 24 hour-partitions. **Also corrects an earlier reading of the same numbers** that took 'Bronze is expensive' to mean the write was expensive and the cluster saturated; the write is not expensive, the read is serialized. Written up in `docs/findings/2026-09-02-bronze-is-single-threaded.md` **as a diagnosis, not a measurement** (§13): the confirming observation — Spark UI task count per Bronze stage, predicted to be exactly 1 per hour-file — has not been taken. The fix, for a later scoped task with its own before-and-after: one `spark.read.text([24 paths])` instead of 24 calls, `event_hour` from `_metadata.file_path`, one per-day `replaceWhere` — which keeps recovery granularity unchanged, since the checkpoint is already day-grained. **Deliberately not applied mid-run**: restarting a healthy 65/92 run would discard ~3.5 h of completed work to chase an unmeasured improvement on a credit that expires Sep 24. |
| 2026-09-02 | Phase 2 burn — sixth real run: the retry fix fails identically, and defect #8 turns out to be a URI scheme, not a timing lag | Triggered `databricks_job.backfill` for real (run `156595311273405`); `databricks jobs get-run-output` on the failed task; `databricks fs ls --long` on the staging volume hours after the fact; read the Databricks volumes docs on URI schemes against the error string; TDD: three new unit tests in `tests/unit/test_spark_path.py`; `make lint` + `mypy --strict` + full suite (205 tests) | **The retry fix failed in exactly the same way, 67 seconds in, which is what finally identified the real defect.** Same `[PATH_NOT_FOUND] ... file:/Volumes/almanac_dbx/burn/staging/2025-07-01-0.json.gz`. Two facts killed the read-after-write-lag hypothesis outright: the deployed wheel demonstrably contained the retry (the traceback runs through `day.py:123` to `_land_hour` to `day.py:151`), and all 24 staged files were still sitting on the volume *hours* later — so no amount of waiting was ever going to make that path resolve. Root cause: **the `file:` scheme**. Databricks defines `file:/` as access relative to the driver's local root, resolved by the JVM's `LocalFileSystem`; a Unity Catalog volume must be handed to Spark as the bare `/Volumes/...` path so it routes through the UC filesystem (every Spark example in the volumes docs uses the bare path; `file:/` appears nowhere for volumes). The driver's *Python* process reaches `/Volumes` via the FUSE mount, which is why all 24 downloads succeeded and the files were provably there — Spark's JVM was looking somewhere else entirely. **Defect #7's own fix is what caused this**: `.as_uri()` was added for defect #5, when staging was genuinely local disk and a bare path wrongly resolved against `dbfs:/`; moving staging onto a volume inverted the requirement, two files apart, with no shared symbol for a type checker or a test to connect. Fixed with `almanac.burn.day.spark_path()` — bare under `/Volumes` (`is_relative_to`, not `startswith`, so a local `/Volumes_backup` isn't mistaken for the mount), `.as_uri()` otherwise — keeping both lessons instead of trading one for the other. The retry, `Settings.max_land_attempts` / `land_retry_seconds`, and its three tests were **removed**: written for a transient the evidence no longer supports, and keeping them would have embedded the wrong explanation in a load-bearing comment. Three new unit tests pin both branches and the lookalike-prefix case; the existing hostile-`fs.defaultFS` integration test still covers defect #5's branch end to end. **The process lesson, recorded because it cost a paid run:** the retry tested a hypothesis instead of establishing which component was failing — exactly the loop `CLAUDE.md` warns about. Reading the scheme in the error string against the vendor docs for that storage type was free and would have settled it before run 6. Defect #8 rewritten in `docs/findings/2026-09-02-burn-deploy-and-first-run-defects.md`. Seventh trigger pending a go-ahead. |
| 2026-09-02 | Phase 2 burn — **the Tier 3 backfill completed**, and the successful run reported FAILED | Triggered `databricks_job.backfill` (run `227270110474807`) on a green tree (`97400ba`, wheel verified to contain `spark_path`); watched 5 h to terminal state via per-day checkpoint polling; read `jobs get-run-output`; derived cost from `get-run` durations against `2026-09-01-azure-pricing.md`; TDD for defect #9 (`tests/unit/test_cli.py`) | **92 of 92 days, 341,060,851 Bronze rows, 341,060,789 clean, 0 quarantined, 165.987 GB gz, zero missing hours across 2,208 files.** Cost **$11.96** — 5.047 billed cluster-hours at $2.370/hr — against a $31.71 estimate: **38% of estimate, 6.5% of the $184 credit**. Per-day 197.5 s vs the calibration's 523.5 s, 2.65x faster *while also running Silver*, which the calibration did not: fetch fell 174.3 s → ~13 s (the `fetch_concurrency: 8` tuning) and 111 s of cluster setup amortised across 92 days instead of 1. Cost inherits the *assumed* 0.75 DBU/node-hour from the pricing doc (§13); everything else measured. **Defect #9, the opposite shape to every earlier one:** the run wrote all 92 days, printed its full JSON summary, then reported FAILED with `SystemExit: 0` — `sys.exit(main())` with `main()` returning 0, and Databricks runs a `spark_python_task` inside IPython, where `SystemExit` propagates as an exception regardless of code (the log carries IPython's own `To exit: use 'exit', 'quit', or Ctrl-D` warning). Succeeding while reporting failure is the safer direction but still wrong: an orchestrator, a retry policy, or a demo reads job status, not logs. Fixed with new `almanac.cli.run_cli(main)` — non-zero exits, zero returns normally — applied at all three entrypoints that can run as a job task (`backfill.py`, `photon_ab.py`, `gold/runner.py`; `photon_ab` had never run, so it would have reported FAILED on success too), and deliberately not to the three local-only scripts, same reasoning that left `build_silver_fixture.py` alone for defect #5. Four tests assert success does not raise and that 1/2/255 still exit with that code, so the fix cannot swallow a real failure. **Left open and explicitly undiagnosed:** 7 staging files (`2025-09-30` hours 3–9, ~600 MB) survived `_clear_downloads`, on the final day only. A write-after-clear race and delete-path consistency lag are both ruled out; cluster-shutdown truncation fits (it predicts last-day-only, which is what was seen) and a lazy `Path.glob()` mutated mid-iteration does not (it predicts scattered leftovers; 91 of 92 days cleared perfectly). **The one-word `list()` fix was deliberately not shipped** — that would repeat defect #8's exact mistake of committing a plausible fix for an unconfirmed cause. The findings doc names the observation that would settle it. |
| 2026-09-02 | Phase 2 Task 8 — throughput published per layer, closing the Bronze-only risk | Read run `227270110474807`'s printed `BackfillReport.summary` for per-layer wall time and `jobs get-run` `setup_duration`/`execution_duration` for billed cluster time; recomputed the report's own `gb_per_bronze_hour`/`gb_per_silver_hour` independently; composed the three layer rates as reciprocals against the run total | **Measured, and the carried-forward risk resolved in the opposite direction.** Whole run **32.89 GB gz per billed cluster-hour** (33.76 execution-only) for Bronze **+** Silver, against Phase 1's **13.84** for Bronze **alone** — **2.4x better while doing strictly more work**, so the §4.5 warning that "if the full pipeline is worse than Bronze-only, Tier 3 shrinks" never bound. Per layer: fetch **462.2** GB/cluster-h (7.3% of execution), Bronze **52.96** (63.8%), Silver **119.64** (28.2%), 0.7% unattributed. The accounting closes as a check, not a claim: the three layer rates compose as reciprocals to 34.01 GB/h against an actual 33.76, the gap being exactly the unattributed 128 s. Gain attributed to fetch parallelism (`fetch_concurrency: 8`; the calibration spent a third of billed time single-threaded on HTTP) plus 471 s of setup amortized over 92 days instead of 1 — both present, **decomposition not measured**. The calibration's 523.5 s "billed" is smaller than this run's setup alone, so the two may not count provisioning alike; the comparison is therefore given on both bases and is 2.4x either way. `2026-09-02-backfill-throughput-per-layer.md`. |
| 2026-09-02 | Phase 2 — is 0 quarantined of 341M rows a clean dataset or a dead ruleset? | Read `conf/sources/gharchive.yml` against `quality.py`; audited what every existing quality test actually asserts; added 6 violation cases + a baseline + a completeness guard to `tests/unit/test_quality.py`; `uv run pytest tests/unit/test_quality.py` | **A real gap, found and closed.** Every prior quality test proved the *mechanism* using rules defined in the test file; the one test touching the shipped ruleset asserted only that each rule **resolves**, discarding the result — so a rule that parsed, bound and never fired passed it. **No test proved any shipped rule had ever quarantined anything**, which made 0 quarantined unreadable: clean data and an inert ruleset produce the same number. Six cases now corrupt one Silver column each and assert the exact set of rules that trips; **all five reject rules fire**, 17 tests green. So the 0 is a fact about the data. **Carve-out, and it matters:** `created_at_not_future` (`created_at <= ingested_at`) cannot discriminate on a backfill at all — every Q3 2025 row is ~1 year older than `ingested_at`, so it fires only on a future-dated corrupt timestamp, never on the skew case it guards. **Four rules had power on this run, not five.** Also honest about reach: all five test *presence*, not correctness, so "no structurally malformed rows" is supported and "no bad data" is not. **Bonus, unasked for:** the 62-row bronze-to-clean gap is dedup, **1 duplicate in 5.5M** — the first measurement of §12 trap 4 on real data at scale, which Phase 0 explicitly could not test (non-adjacent hours) and Phase 1 covered only by unit test. `2026-09-02-zero-quarantined.md`. |
| 2026-09-02 | Phase 2 Task 9 — Photon A/B pre-flight: a stale deploy and a concurrent-arm race, both caught before spending | `databricks workspace export` on the deployed `scripts/photon_ab.py`; `uv build` + `databricks workspace import --overwrite` for the wheel and both scripts; `workspace get-status` for a stored-size check; read `databricks.tf`'s task blocks for `depends_on`; `terraform fmt`/`validate`/`plan`/`apply`; `databricks jobs get` against the live job; `jobs run-now` | **Two defects caught before a paid run, neither visible locally.** (1) **The deployed code was stale.** `photon_ab.py` on the workspace was still pre-`a37bc7d` — `sys.exit(main())` — so this job's *first successful* execution would have reported FAILED, defect #9 again, on a job that has never run and therefore has no prior green to compare against. Rebuilt the wheel (now carries `almanac/cli.py`) and re-uploaded it plus both scripts; deployed `photon_ab.py` verified to end `run_cli(main)`. Wheel verified by **stored size** (37,421 bytes, matching local exactly) because checksum-by-download is unavailable here: `workspace export --format RAW` is refused (`Invalid export request: format=RAW, directDownload=false`) and the plain export mangles binary, returning 41,494 bytes — 4,073 extra — and an unreadable zip. The upload path is fine; the *export* path is what corrupts, which is worth knowing before anyone trusts a downloaded artifact. Re-confirmed the recorded rule that the wheel must go via `workspace import --overwrite`, never `databricks sync` (`dist/.gitignore` is `*`, so sync skips exactly the file that matters). (2) **The two arms race on staging.** `arm_standard` and `arm_photon` carry **no `depends_on`** — verified against the live job, not just the config — so Databricks runs them concurrently, and both were pointed at the same `--staging-dir` while measuring the same calibration day. `fetch_hour` does not skip an existing file; it re-downloads and does `write_bytes`/`replace` on the same `2025-08-13-N.json.gz.part`, from two clusters, on a shared FUSE volume, while the other's Spark may be reading it. Likely presentation is a torn gzip read surfacing as `PATH_NOT_FOUND` — **indistinguishable at a glance from defect #8**, which cost five round trips. Fixed with a staging subdir per arm; `terraform plan` showed **0 to add, 1 to change, 0 to destroy**, touching only the two `--staging-dir` values, which also re-confirms committed config still matches live state. Quota re-checked live rather than assumed: two concurrent 5-VM clusters need 40 of 48 available vCPUs. Run `674441653017831` triggered. |
| 2026-09-02 | Phase 2 Task 9 — the Gold layer could not address cloud storage; A/B arm 1 died on it | Triggered `databricks_job.photon_ab` (run `674441653017831`); `databricks jobs get-run-output` on the failed task and read the full traceback for the almanac frames; reproduced `Path('abfss://...')` locally; swept every `argparse type=Path` across all entrypoints; `ruff`, `ruff format`, `mypy --strict` (66 files) | **Predicted before the run failed, then confirmed by the traceback rather than assumed.** `arm_standard` FAILED with `AnalysisException: Missing cloud file system scheme`, and the frames name the chain exactly: `photon_ab.py:44` `Path(ctx.paths.silver)` → `runner.py:73` `register_silver_sources` → `sources.py:26` `CREATE TABLE ... LOCATION`. **`pathlib` is the entire defect.** `Path('abfss://silver@acct/events')` collapses the `//` to `abfss:/silver@acct/events`, which is then **no longer absolute**, so the old `.resolve()` anchored it under the driver's cwd — two silent corruptions producing a plausible string and an external table that fails only at first read. **This is the third instance of one class**, after defect #7 (bare local path resolving against `fs.defaultFS`) and #8 (`file://` on a UC volume): each time, a path decision correct on local disk and silently wrong against cloud storage. `spark_path()` fixed where #8 bit, not the class. **Structurally uncatchable here**: every Gold test drives the CLI with `str(tmp_path)`, a local path where `Path` is the right type, so the suite, `mypy --strict` and CI are all blind to a bug whose only trigger is a URI appearing nowhere but Terraform. Fixed as a **type asymmetry, not a cast** — `warehouse` is `str` (may be a URI), `metastore` stays `Path` (Derby genuinely is a local file), and `table_location()` passes URIs through while still resolving relative local paths, preserving the invariant the old `.resolve()` existed for. Six tests, two load-bearing: the emitted SQL keeps `abfss://` intact, and `str(Path(uri)) != uri` is pinned for abfss/s3/gs so the `str` route cannot be read as style and reverted. **Class swept, not just the instance**: every remaining `type=Path` takes a real filesystem path (`/Volumes` FUSE staging/checkpoints/out, `/local_disk0` metastores, workspace project dirs) and none receives a lake URI. `arm_photon` was cancelled while still waiting for a cluster, having done no work; the same defect would have taken out the Gold run too, since it shares `register_silver_sources`. |
| 2026-09-03 | Phase 2 — a Gold job, so the models meet the real quarter | Read `process_day` to confirm what the backfill actually lands; `databricks workspace list`/`export` on the synced `src/`; wrote `scripts/gold.py` + `databricks_job.gold`; `terraform fmt`/`validate`; `make check` | **Found that Gold had never touched real data, and closed the gap.** `process_day` is fetch → Bronze → Silver and stops, and Terraform carried only `backfill` and `photon_ab` — so the $11.96 and 341,060,851 rows bought two layers **nothing downstream ever read**, with `dim_repo` / `fact_pull_request` / `agg_repo_daily` built only over 2,000-row fixtures. `scripts/gold.py` is a launch shim in the same shape as the other two and carries no logic, since `almanac.gold.runner` is already a complete CLI. **The alternative was rejected on evidence**: pointing `python_file` at the synced `src/almanac/gold/runner.py` would execute a file whose imports resolve from the installed wheel — two copies of one contract — and the synced `src/` was verified **stale** (still `sys.exit(main())`, no `cli.py`), which is exactly that hazard already realised. Metastore deliberately ephemeral on `/local_disk0`, and correct rather than merely convenient: a **first** full build has nothing to be incremental from. It is **not** correct for a second run — `dim_repo` is a dbt snapshot, so SCD2's multi-version path needs catalog state outliving the cluster, and choosing that store (Unity Catalog vs Derby-over-FUSE, still unverified for file locking) is **its own decision, recorded as open rather than defaulted into**. Also generalised defect #9 from three fixed call sites into a test: every script a `spark_python_task` names must exit through `run_cli`, asserted against source, because the defect lives in the line that never runs under test — `photon_ab.py` carried `sys.exit(main())` unnoticed precisely because it had never run as a job. |
| 2026-09-03 | Phase 2 Task 9 — the A/B's dbt step pointed at a relative project dir; caught pre-flight | Read `_run`'s `GoldTarget` construction against `cli_flags`'s defaults; cancelled run `488000457742954` while both arms were still `PENDING`; 3 new tests in `tests/unit/test_gold_runner.py`; `terraform apply`; `databricks jobs get` to verify live params; `databricks external-locations list` | **Second certain failure caught before it cost anything.** `photon_ab.py` built `GoldTarget` with only `warehouse` and `metastore`, so `project_dir` fell back to `DEFAULT_PROJECT_DIR` — the **relative** `Path("dbt")`. A job task's cwd is not the repo root, which this project established the expensive way as **defect #3** (`--source-config`'s relative default, `FileNotFoundError` before the first run read a byte — the reason Terraform passes that path explicitly). dbt would have failed to find `dbt_project.yml` *after* both arms redid all of Bronze and Silver. Cancelled while both arms were `PENDING`: **zero compute spent**. **A second footgun behind the first:** `profiles_dir` does **not** follow `project_dir` — setting only the latter leaves `--profiles-dir` on the same relative default — so both are now passed, with a test pinning that behaviour and a third asserting `DEFAULT_PROJECT_DIR` is relative so the reason stays legible. `--target-path` and `DBT_LOG_PATH` moved to `/local_disk0` for the same underlying cause: both default under `--project-dir`, a workspace path the driver cannot write to. Verified live before re-triggering, not just in config: `--project-dir → /Workspace/Shared/almanac/dbt`, `--target-path → /local_disk0/dbt_target`, `DBT_LOG_PATH` set, per-arm staging intact. **One UC worry checked and cleared rather than assumed**: `external-locations list` shows all four lake containers (bronze/silver/gold/features) registered against the `almanac-lake` storage credential, so `CREATE TABLE ... LOCATION 'abfss://...'` is addressable. The remaining unknown is recorded rather than guessed: `_session()` returns the cluster's live session and `dbt_session`'s `getOrCreate()` therefore **ignores** the warehouse/metastore configs — the trap `runner.py`'s own docstring names — which on a cluster with a real metastore may simply be fine. |
| 2026-09-03 | Phase 2 Task 9 — **the Photon A/B ran, both arms SUCCESS**, and its §8.2 hypothesis is falsified | Triggered `databricks_job.photon_ab` (run `693303490917119`); retrieved both `ArmMeasurement` JSONs from `/Volumes/almanac_dbx/burn/photon_ab/`; read `jobs get-run-output` for both dbt logs; `databricks tables list` on `almanac_dbx.silver`/`.gold` after the run | **Measured, and one row of three is not usable — which the catalog listing, not the run, revealed.** Both arms report `compressed_gb = 2.0124815106391907`, byte-identical, so the slice is genuinely common. Bronze **1.18x** (213.9→180.6 s), Silver **1.96x** (63.4→32.3 s), Gold 1.45x (60.9→42.1 s); both arms `PASS=23 WARN=0 ERROR=0`. **`hypothesis_holds` is False.** §8.2 predicted Bronze would be helped "little or nothing" because it is "almost all JSON parsing" — but Bronze measured above the 1.10 materiality threshold, **and the premise was wrong about the code**: `_land_bronze` is `read.text` + two literal columns + a Delta write and parses no JSON at all (that is Silver's `parse_events`). Recorded as falsified rather than reworded, per §13. **Gold's 1.45x is discarded, not quoted.** Both arms shared one Unity Catalog metastore concurrently: `register_silver_sources`'s `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` let the photon arm win, so `silver.events` points at `photon_ab_photon/clean` and **the standard arm's dbt read the photon arm's data** while its own Silver output sat ignored; and `gold.*` are MANAGED tables both arms `merge`-ed into simultaneously. Bronze/Silver were cleanly isolated per arm and stand. **Not fixed in this commit** — a per-arm dbt schema changes what the arms write and so needs its own run to produce a comparable number; shipping the fix unmeasured is defect #8's mistake. **Cost verdict withheld by construction**: `dbus_consumed` is 0.0 in both arms (the job passes no `--dbus`), and since Photon's entire penalty *is* the DBU premium, a cost verdict computed from VM time alone would systematically favour Photon. Closing §8.2 needs billing-system DBU totals for task runs `1063658033177813`/`719412933113624`, then `compare --usd-per-dbu 0.30 --usd-per-node-hour 0.474 --num-nodes 5` (the measured `D4ds_v6` westus3 rate, not the docstring's 0.249 placeholder). **Four things cleared incidentally** — first Gold run outside a laptop/CI: the `abfss://` fix works end to end, the explicit `--project-dir` fix works, `getOrCreate()` **does** drop the warehouse/metastore configs as `runner.py` warns but is harmless against a real UC metastore (previously unverified, now observed), and `run_cli` reported SUCCESS on a normally-completing task — the exact case that used to report FAILED. `2026-09-03-photon-ab.md`. |
| 2026-09-03 | Phase 2 — **Gold built on the real quarter**, and the stale pointer that would have faked it | Dropped 6 stale UC objects via `databricks tables delete` (a metadata op, no compute); triggered `databricks_job.gold` (run `655249155948620`); read the dbt log; `databricks tables get` afterwards to confirm what was actually read | **PASS=23 WARN=0 ERROR=0 in 678.21 s of dbt, for $0.78** (382 s setup + 803 s execution = 0.329 cluster-hours) — 0.4% of the credit. First time `dim_repo` / `fact_pull_request` / `agg_repo_daily` met anything but the 2,000-row fixtures, since `process_day` lands Bronze+Silver only. **Three tests are the project's premises, not dbt hygiene**, and all now hold at 341M-row scale: `assert_label_point_in_time` (no label computed from anything after the prediction point — the property the project exists for), `assert_label_exclusion_accounts_for_every_row` (conservation, the Gold-boundary echo of Silver's `clean + quarantined == input`), and `relationships_fact_pull_request_repo_id__repo_id__ref_dim_repo_` (referential integrity across the real repo population). Enforced `not_null` contracts passed too, so a wrong output *type* at scale would have reddened the build. **The near-miss is the finding.** `register_silver_sources` uses `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, and `almanac_dbx.silver.events` already pointed at `photon_ab_photon/clean` — the A/B arm's **one-day 2 GB slice**. A "real quarter" build against it would have resolved to one day, built every model, **passed all 23 tests** (they assert invariants, not volume) and reported SUCCESS in an identical log. `gold.*` were MANAGED tables holding both arms' contaminated output that an `incremental`/`merge` build would have merged into rather than replaced. Dropped all six first; EXTERNAL `silver.*` lost only pointers, data untouched. **Verified after the run rather than assumed**: `silver.events -> abfss://silver@.../events/clean`, the 92-partition quarter. **Still unexercised**: SCD2's multi-version path — `dim_repo` is a dbt snapshot, a first build captures only initial versions, and Phase 0's 3,792 renames need a second run over a different window plus a metastore outliving the cluster (an open decision, not defaulted into). Generalisable: **`IF NOT EXISTS` at a metastore boundary is a silent read redirection, not a no-op, and it fails in the direction that looks like success** — the second time this shape cost something in two days. `2026-09-03-gold-on-the-real-quarter.md`. |
| 2026-09-03 | Phase 2 Task 9 — per-arm schemas, so the A/B's Gold row can be measured at all | Parameterised the Silver source schema (`runner.default_source_schema` + `dbt/models/sources.yml`) and the Gold target schema (`dbt/profiles.yml`) on env vars; per-arm values in `databricks.tf`; 4 new tests; a real local `dbt build` (145 s) to prove the templating still resolves to the plain defaults; `make check` (236 tests, was 233); `terraform apply`; `databricks jobs get` to verify live | **The fix states the schema name once, which is the point.** Run `693303490917119`'s Gold row was unusable because both arms shared `silver`/`gold` in one metastore concurrently. Isolation needs the *creating* side (`register_silver_sources`) and the *resolving* side (dbt's `source()`) to agree, and those are two different files — so both now read `ALMANAC_SILVER_SCHEMA`, with `ALMANAC_GOLD_SCHEMA` doing the same for the target schema, matching the pattern the `databricks` profile target already used. Disagreement would mean dbt resolving a table nothing registered. Terraform sets `ab_standard_*` / `ab_photon_*`, which isolates the arms from each other **and** keeps them clear of the real `silver`/`gold` the gold job owns — so a future A/B can no longer strand a stale pointer where Gold expects the quarter, which is exactly what cost this session a near-miss. Verified live rather than in config: the two clusters carry distinct env vars. Local/CI unaffected — the `env_var` defaults keep every non-cluster run on `silver`/`gold`, proven by a real dbt build rather than by reading the template. Re-run `325049271562727` triggered. |
| 2026-09-03 | Phase 2 Task 9 — **the A/B at n=3**, and a correction: "falsified" was over-claimed | Two more replicate runs (`325049271562727`, `236314776904974`); pulled both arm JSONs after each (the job overwrites the same two paths); aggregated with a range rule **fixed before replicate 3 was run**; computed a break-even DBU multiplier against the backfill's measured shape; rewrote `compare_arms` to take replicates; `make check` | **Two rows stand, one cannot be called, and the harness was reporting a verdict it could not support.** Speedups across 3 replicate pairs — Bronze **1.18 / 1.05 / 1.23** (mean 1.15), Silver **1.96 / 2.40 / 2.06** (mean **2.14**), Gold **1.45 / 1.29 / 1.42** (mean **1.38**); all six arms SUCCESS, all three pairs byte-identical on `compressed_gb`. Silver and Gold clear the 1.10 threshold on every replicate and are the defensible results. **Bronze is withheld**: its range straddles, and the mean clearing 1.10 is exactly the case the pre-committed range rule exists to refuse — switching to the mean after seeing the data is the move the rule prevents. **The reason is the noise floor**: re-running an *identical* arm spreads 2.3–29.9% (photon bronze 19.9%, standard gold 29.9%), so the ~15% Bronze effect is smaller than the noise of the arms measuring it. **Correction to the row above and to `2026-09-03-photon-ab.md`:** that document previously said the hypothesis was **falsified**, on run `693303490917119` alone. Run `325049271562727` computed the opposite verdict from the same code and slice. The honest statement is neither — **the measurement never had the power to support either**, and "refuted" is a stronger claim than "cannot tell". **Harness defect, this project's own threshold-vs-distribution mistake:** `hypothesis_holds` returned a `bool` from n=1, and `paid_for_itself` returned `true` from `dbus_consumed = 0.0` (cost collapses to VM time, and Photon's whole penalty *is* its DBU premium, so it systematically favoured Photon). Both now refuse the question — `compare_arms` takes replicates, verdicts may be `indeterminate`, `hypothesis_holds` is `bool | None`, `paid_for_itself` is `None` without DBU totals, and `_run_cost` averages so replicate count cannot inflate cost. **Decision: do not enable Photon.** The unanswerable cost question was inverted into a break-even DBU multiplier against the backfill's real shape — **1.55 (Bronze no help) / 1.96 (mean) / 2.16 (best case)** — which straddles Photon's ~2x multiplier, and keeps straddling (1.79–2.28) across a plausible 0.5–1.0 DBU/node-hour range, so it does not rest on that assumed input. **A wash, and the leg that would decide it is the leg that cannot be measured.** The real lever is Bronze's single-threaded gzip: 63.8% of execution, the layer Photon helps least, and fixing it would also shrink the layer that makes this question ambiguous. **Not buying more replicates** — the variance is confounded (arms run concurrently with 441 s vs 111 s provisioning), so more n buys precision on a biased estimate; controlling the overlap is the fix. |

Over-stated: **the SCD2 metastore decision is not a Phase 2 gate item** — the gate asks for SCD2 *verified* (rename closes the old row, one current row, case-only renames, double rename → three versions) and Task 3's tests satisfy it; running the multi-version path on real data at scale was an addition of mine and belongs to Phase 3+. **The taxonomy was written from the SQL and that caught an error**: `open_unobserved` fires on a null `opened_at`, not on "still open" as the summary implied — documented precisely, with the `right_censored` vs `closed_no_response` distinction spelled out because collapsing "we do not know yet" into "there is no answer" would corrupt the trainable population. Diagram: Gold → `done`; **`API` deliberately left `todo`** — the REST source is built and tested but never lands in Delta, so that edge is still aspirational and marking it done would be a false claim. **§13: one item closed, one qualified.** Adjacent-hour duplicate rate is **measured at last** — 62 in 341,060,851, 1 in 5.5M across 2,208 consecutive files, the trap-4 gap Phase 0 explicitly could not test. Photon's per-layer effect marked `[~]`: wall-clock measured, DBUs still open. **DBUs are blocked for two concrete reasons, now recorded rather than left as "not done yet":** `system.billing` is not exposed on this metastore and enabling it returns `User is not an account admin for Account`; and Azure Cost Management returns `Microsoft.Databricks` rows tagged with exactly the right key (`ClusterId`, `BillingOriginProduct: JOBS`) but with `usageQuantity` and `pretaxCost` **null**, still lagging. **A structural finding that outlives both:** "DBUs per layer" is not measurable at all — DBUs bill per cluster-hour and the three layers share one cluster sequentially, so any per-layer figure is the run total apportioned by wall-clock share, i.e. arithmetic over numbers already published, not a meter reading. |

Cross-checked from raw DBUs, not just through the rate ($6.28 VM + $8.34 DBU). Tier 3 stays 7.9% of the credit against a 40% cap, so no tiering decision moves. **DBU consumption is workload-shaped, not node-shaped** (backfill 1.101 vs Gold 0.883/node-hour) — a single figure must not be quoted across shapes, the same error as quoting Bronze-only throughput for the full pipeline. **The Photon verdict hardens from "a wash" to a measured loss.** Multiplier **2.297** (2.118 / 2.381 / 2.394 across replicates), and the earlier break-even table was **arithmetically wrong** — it used `V = $0.474` as the VM rate, but that is VM ($0.249) *plus assumed DBU*, double-counting DBUs into `V` while also charging them through `d`. Corrected break-even **1.31 / 1.54 / 1.65**; the measured multiplier clears every one, and so does the lowest single replicate. Harness on real data: `dbu_overhead 2.297`, `cost_off $0.40`, `cost_on $0.74`, **`paid_for_itself: False`** — 1.85x the cost for a 1.31x speedup. Both errors (inflated `V`, assumed ~2x multiplier) happened to **flatter** Photon, which is why the recommendation survived them. `hypothesis_holds` stays `None`: the cost question became answerable, the Bronze speedup did not, and the two claims stayed independent — which is what the harness rewrite was for. **Access cost two confident wrong diagnoses, both recorded**: `system.billing` was never disabled (every schema reports `MANAGED`; the earlier "only `ai` and `information_schema`" was a **permissions filter on a non-admin identity**), and the `#EXT#` UPN is Entra's internal representation of a guest, not a sign-in name, so no password could ever have worked. The real blocker was `AADSTS500200` — the tenant's only Global Administrator was a personal Microsoft account, which the account console refuses. **Still not measurable: per-layer DBUs** — one cluster, three sequential layers, so any per-layer figure is the run total apportioned by wall-clock share. `2026-09-03-measured-dbus.md`. |

Both fixed together with one change: a `_qualifies` boolean computed per candidate (`coalesce(event_time < as_of_timestamp, false)`), ranked so a qualifying row always outranks a non-qualifying one, and feature columns explicitly nulled when the row that won the ranking doesn't qualify — so every spine row survives the join, and a future-only or absent history reads as "unknown", never as a dropped row or a leaked value. |

A feature vector is built as-of `T` from version `v1`; a "late arrival" event (`event_time < T`, but appended as a real second Delta version well after `v1`, not backdated in place) lands as `v2`; re-querying `v1` reproduces the original result byte-for-byte, while re-querying live (`v2`) is still leak-free (`event_time < T` holds) but silently changes the answer — exactly why a training set pins its Delta version at build time rather than re-deriving "current" later. The runner integration test writes all three v1 tables from real Silver-shaped Delta input and confirms a second full run overwrites rather than duplicates (full recompute is idempotent by construction), and exercises `main()` for real — `SparkSession.getActiveSession()` picks up the test's own session, so no mocking was needed to prove the CLI wiring works. **One test-only defect found by the *whole-repo* `mypy src tests`, not the scoped per-task check**: `test_features_groups.py`'s `_row` helper had zero parameter annotations, invisible to Task 3's narrower `mypy --strict src/almanac/features/bot.py src/almanac/features/groups.py` because that scoped run never touched the test file — fixed with a `_Row` type alias, and the plan document updated to carry this fix so a future reader isn't surprised by it.
