| 2026-09-04 | Phase 4 Task 9 (in progress) — `compute_author_activity`'s self-join is O(N²) per author, found at real scale | Three `databricks_job.build_features` runs over the real 341M-row Silver quarter (`924195167084181`, `48552036331781`, `485360890184517`), each hung in the same Delta write stage; a live executor thread dump and the AQE physical plan from the third; storage-account `Transactions` metrics over both hang windows. Then the fix: 3 new tests (RED on the burst case), reformulated `compute_author_activity`, `make check` (whole repo, 277 tests, 14:09), a rebuilt wheel, and a fourth `build_features` run. | **Root-caused and fixed — a Phase 3 bug only a real-scale run could surface. The fixed run then completed in 437 s of execution.** Task 9 needs the v1 feature tables materialised on the lake (Phase 3 ran offline against fixtures), so a `databricks_job.build_features` was added and run. It hung every time writing `author_activity`: ~10 of 17 write tasks finished in seconds, ~7 (the ones whose `author_login` hash held a bot login) never did. **Ruled out with evidence** — not Storage throttling (zero `ServerBusyError` on the account), not GC (7–10 s total), not OOM, not a lock (stuck threads `RUNNABLE`). **The cause**: the live thread dump showed the stuck threads spinning in `BytesToBytesMap.safeLookup` under a `HashAggregate`, and the AQE plan reported `Statistics(sizeInBytes=596.7 TiB, isRuntime=true)` on the `SortMergeJoin` feeding it — `compute_author_activity`'s `this ⋈ prior` self-join on `author_login` is a near-Cartesian blow-up for `dependabot[bot]` and friends, and `spark.sql.adaptive.skewJoin` cannot split a self-join. **The fix**: reformulated as a time-ordered running aggregate over each author's own open/close events (a resolution at each `closed_at`, a query at each `opened_at`, ordered so a query precedes a same-instant resolution — the strict `<`), read through `rowsBetween(unboundedPreceding, currentRow)`. O(N log N), skew-proof, the exact shape `compute_repo_activity` already uses. **One behaviour change toward correctness**: the old `GROUP BY (author_login, opened_at)` over the join counted each prior once per PR opened at that same timestamp — a bot opening K PRs in one second got `prior_pr_count = K × true` (rate unaffected). The new form counts once; pinned by a test that fails on the old code. Strict-boundary and per-author scoping unchanged, now each has its own test; the plan's original worked example is untouched and green. `docs/findings/2026-09-04-author-activity-self-join.md`. **The first two hung runs also carried a behaviour-neutral `cluster_log_conf`** (log delivery to `almanac_dbx.burn.cluster_logs`) added to the `build_features` and `train_model` job clusters — diagnostics before a fix, per the project's rule for an unattributable failure. Cost of the three cancelled feature builds: ≈$13 of the trial credit. |
| 2026-09-04 | Phase 4 Task 9 (in progress) — Gold's fact is a Unity Catalog table on the workspace, not a Delta path | The first training run (`961660350236501`) failed in `build_training_frame`; `databricks tables get almanac_dbx.gold.fact_pull_request`; the fix — `dataset.py`/`runner.py`/terraform + 4 test files re-pointed at a table name; `pytest` on the 6 model dataset/runner tests, then full `make check`. | **A local-vs-cloud divergence, same class as the backfill's `/local_disk0` and `file:` URI defects.** `[PATH_NOT_FOUND] abfss://gold@.../warehouse/gold.db/fact_pull_request` — `build_training_frame` read Gold's fact as path Delta, the layout the local Gold tests use. On the workspace the Gold job's dbt `session` run creates `gold.fact_pull_request` **unqualified**, and the cluster's Unity Catalog default catalog resolves that to the managed table `almanac_dbx.gold.fact_pull_request` in the metastore's own storage — not under the job's `--warehouse` abfss path, which is a dead parameter there (confirmed: the table is `MANAGED`, `storage_location` is the UC managed path, last written by the one successful Gold run). **Fix**: `build_training_frame` reads Gold's fact **by name** (`gold_table: str`, `spark.read.table(...)`, still `versionAsOf`-pinnable) — the more correct design, since a dbt model *is* a metastore table. Silver `/clean` and the three feature tables stay path reads: they are hand-written path Delta (`df.write...save(path)`), never registered. The model integration tests now register their tmp Delta dir as an external table so the by-name read is exercised for real, uniquely named per test (the `spark` fixture is session-scoped). `docs/findings/2026-09-04-gold-is-a-metastore-table-not-a-path.md`. Training re-run pending on the rebuilt wheel. |
| 2026-09-04 | Phase 4 Task 9 (in progress) — the same O(N²) bot skew, now in `as_of_join` itself | The re-run training job (`792359179723419`) ran 102 min then was cancelled; its live SQL plan; the `as_of_join` rewrite + 1 new test; `pytest` on the join/assemble/leakage/model suites, then full `make check`. | **The feature platform's core primitive had the same blow-up, and this one is the consequential fix.** With `compute_author_activity` fixed the feature-build ran clean (437 s), but the training job then hung in `build_training_frame` → `assemble_training_set` → `as_of_join(spine, author_activity, on=["author_login"])`. Live SQL plan: `SortMergeJoin(skew=true) LeftOuter` (spine 1.18E7 ⋈ author_activity 1.32E7 on `author_login`) → `WindowGroupLimit row_number()==1` → `Exchange Statistics(sizeInBytes=11.7 PiB, isRuntime=true)`. `as_of_join` was `spine.join(feature_table, on)` then `row_number().over(partitionBy(row_id))` — it materialises every (spine × feature) pair for a key before ranking throws all but one away, and `dependabot[bot]` carries ~10⁵ rows on **both** sides. **Fix**: union spine rows (query events at `as_of_timestamp`) and feature rows (value events at `event_time`), one timeline per `on` key, query sorted ahead of a value at an equal instant (the strict `<`), carry the latest value row forward with `last(struct(...), ignorenulls=True)` over `rowsBetween(unboundedPreceding, currentRow)` — the struct is taken whole, so a legitimately null feature value is kept, not skipped for an older non-null one. O(N log N), one shuffle-and-sort per key. `as_of_join`'s three existing cases (latest-prior-wins, strict boundary, cold start) and the leakage suite all stay green; new `test_one_key_with_many_spine_rows_and_many_feature_rows` pins the skewed-key shape. `docs/findings/2026-09-04-author-activity-self-join.md` (§"The same shape, again"). **Also in this commit**: `databricks_job.train_model` moves from `is_single_node` to the 5-VM shape — the run above spent ~40 min of single-node Spark on `build_training_frame` before the `as_of_join` blow-up even showed, so §5.2's "dataset.py's Spark reads are small" premise was wrong (the spine is a full Silver-quarter scan); the LightGBM fit still runs single-threaded on the driver, so §5.2's "training never distributes" still holds. Training re-run pending on the rebuilt wheel. Cancelled training run: ≈$0.30 (single node). |
| 2026-09-04 | Phase 4 Task 9 — **done: a real, measured null result, not a failure** | Training run `283014697409852` on the fixed, multi-node `train_model` job (12 min: 442 s setup + 288 s exec, vs. the single-node run's 102 min that never finished); `databricks api get` on the MLflow run for `model_mae`/`baseline_mae`/`beats_baseline`; `databricks registered-models get` confirming no version registered; a SQL query over `almanac_dbx.gold.fact_pull_request` (Serverless Starter Warehouse, started for this, stopped after) for the §5.2 SLA-threshold percentile. | **`beats_baseline = False`, measured, not forced.** `model_mae` **108,889.9 s** (≈30.25 h) vs `baseline_mae` **71,916.9 s** (≈19.98 h) — LightGBM with default hyperparameters is ~51% worse than the naive per-segment median. §5.1's own gate does exactly what it exists to do: `almanac_dbx.models.pr_review_sla_risk` does not exist (confirmed directly), so `databricks_model_serving.pr_review_sla_risk` is **not applied** — forcing it around a null result would be exactly the mistake the gate is there to prevent. **Diagnosed, not silently accepted**: the trainable population's `time_to_first_response_seconds` (n = 2,956,518, same `label_exclusion IS NULL` population) is severely right-skewed — p50 **72 s**, p75 **1,487 s**, p90 **76,533 s**, mean **72,054 s**, max **7,921,875 s** (91.7 days). `baseline_mae` sits almost exactly at the mean, consistent with MAE on this shape being decided by the multi-day tail; a default squared-error-style objective pulls LightGBM's predictions up for the ~50% of PRs that resolve in about a minute, which costs more in MAE across millions of fast rows than it recovers on the tail. Recorded as the measured explanation, not applied as a quiet fix — §5.1's "not retried until it flips" holds, and a log-transformed target or a different loss is a stated next iteration, not tonight's number. **§5.2's deferred SLA threshold, closed**: **p75 = 1,487 s (≈25 min)**, computed once from the real trainable population as committed, consumed by no code in this plan. **Provenance verified from the run itself**, not assumed: MLflow's `sparkDatasourceInfo` tag records the exact Delta version of every input read (`events/clean` v91, all three feature tables v0, Gold's fact v1) — the version-pinning discipline Task 7's leakage suite proved is visible end-to-end on a live run. `docs/findings/2026-09-04-model-serving-measured.md`. **Task 9 cost, full window**: three cancelled feature-build runs (≈$13) + one cancelled single-node training run (≈$0.30) + one successful feature build (≈$1) + one successful training run (≈$1) ≈ **$15 of ≈$150** remaining credit, 20 days before the Sep 24 expiry. |
| 2026-09-04 | Phase 4 — closing §5.2's deferred question: classification, before any code | Direct SQL against `almanac_dbx.gold.fact_pull_request` (Serverless Starter Warehouse, started for this, stopped after): the full `label_exclusion` breakdown, a check of the naive "every `closed_no_response` row is a breach" move before adopting it, and the exact breach rate overall and per `is_bot_author`; live-checked `LGBMClassifier` (current, 4.7.0) and MLflow's 2026 classification-evaluation guidance (PR-AUC over ROC-AUC on an imbalanced target) | **§5.3 written, and one naive move caught before it became a decision.** §5.2 chose regression specifically to avoid "an undocumented decision about where `closed_no_response` falls" — Task 9's measured null result is the trigger to answer it now, with real data. **The naive inclusion rule was wrong for 74% of the category**: of 4,363,603 `closed_no_response` rows, only 1,130,833 were open ≥1,487 s (the measured SLA threshold) before closing without response; 3,232,770 closed *faster* than the threshold, so the SLA window never had a chance to be exceeded — labelling all of them a breach would have overstated the positive class by 3.2M rows. Caught by running the check (Gate 1: "the cheapest disconfirming check... run it before concluding"), not by reasoning about it. **Decision**: trainable population becomes `label_exclusion IS NULL OR label_exclusion = 'closed_no_response'` (**7,320,121 rows, 2.48× the regression's 2,956,518**), `breach` derived at training time (no Gold/dbt change) with the two-branch rule above; `right_censored` and `author_unobserved` stay excluded (point-in-time correctness and no-anchor, respectively — both stated in §5.3); the SLA threshold is **reused** at 1,487 s (Task 9's measured p75), not recomputed on the wider population, to keep it a stable constant. **Measured breach rate 25.55% overall**, and **bots breach *more* than humans** (32.16% vs 21.97%) — the opposite of the pre-measurement intuition, stated because it corrected an assumption. Model: `LGBMClassifier`, same ten features. Baseline: per-segment breach *rate*, generalizing `fit_naive_baseline`'s existing shape (`agg` param, `"median"`/`"mean"`) rather than a second dataclass. Gate: PR-AUC (average precision) primary, per MLflow's live-checked 2026 guidance for a 25.55%-positive target; a **comparison sweep** of ≥2 `LGBMClassifier` configs (default, `is_unbalance=True`) logs every candidate to MLflow, registers whichever wins if it beats the baseline. Same registered model name `pr_review_sla_risk` — the regression run never registered anything, so there is no v1 to collide with. Phase 4 continues (Tasks 10–14, `docs/plans/2026-09-04-phase-4-classification-plan.md`); §5.2's regression result is **not deleted** — recorded, cross-referenced, and is the reason this section exists. `docs/design/2026-09-01-almanac-system-design.md` §5.3. No code changed in this commit. |
| 2026-09-04 | Phase 4 Task 10 — `join_breach_label`, and a shared feature-frame builder | `pytest tests/unit/test_model_dataset.py tests/integration/test_model_dataset_versions.py -v` (4 tests, 1 new unit + 1 new integration; both existing tests untouched and still green) | **Green on the first implementation.** `_build_feature_frame` extracted from `build_training_frame` (Silver → spine → `assemble_training_set`, no label) — `build_training_frame` itself is behaviourally unchanged, proven by its own unmodified test staying green. `join_breach_label` implements §5.3's population and derivation exactly: inner-joins `(repo_id, pr_number)` against `label_exclusion IS NULL OR label_exclusion = 'closed_no_response'`, deriving `breach` two ways (`time_to_first_response_seconds > threshold` for the trainable rows, `(closed_at - opened_at) >= threshold` for `closed_no_response`) — one test fixture covers both branches plus all three excluded categories (`author_unobserved`/`right_censored`/`draft`) in a single dict-equality assertion. `build_classification_frame` mirrors `build_training_frame`'s shape and version-pinning, proven end to end against real Delta I/O the same way Task 2's integration test proved the regression path. |
| 2026-09-04 | Phase 4 Task 11 — the breach-rate baseline | `pytest tests/unit/test_model_baseline.py tests/unit/test_model_train.py -q` (7 tests, 2 new; the two pre-existing median tests untouched and green) | **Green, and the generalisation is a real reduction of duplication, not a speculative one.** `fit_naive_baseline` gains `agg: Literal["median", "mean"] = "median"` rather than a second dataclass+function pair — median (regression, §5.1) and mean-of-a-boolean (classification breach rate, §5.3) are the same "segment → statistic → fallback to the overall statistic" shape, checked directly: nothing outside `baseline.py` touches `NaiveBaseline`'s field names, so renaming `medians`/`overall_median` to the generic `values`/`overall_value` is contained to this one file. Default `agg="median"` reproduces both pre-existing tests' results unchanged, proving `train.py`'s regression call site needed no change. |
| 2026-09-04 | Phase 4 Task 12 — `train_classifier`: the comparison sweep and the PR-AUC gate | `pytest tests/unit/test_model_train.py tests/unit/test_model_train_logging.py tests/unit/test_model_baseline.py tests/unit/test_model_dataset.py tests/integration/test_model_dataset_versions.py -q` (17 tests, 5 new; every pre-existing regression-path test untouched and green) | **Green, and a real methodological finding surfaced while writing the no-signal test.** `train_classifier` fits the breach-rate baseline and both `CLASSIFIER_CANDIDATES` (`"default"`, `"is_unbalance"`) on one split, scores each by `roc_auc`/`average_precision`/`log_loss`, and gates on the highest-`average_precision` candidate beating the baseline (`_best_candidate`, its own directly-tested pure function). `log_classification_run` logs every candidate plus the baseline as its own MLflow run in the same experiment. **Measured, not assumed**: a first no-signal test (`is_bot_author` held constant, matching the regression suite's existing fixture shape) asserted `beats_baseline is False` and failed — checked directly at n=400/1,000/2,000/5,000/10,000, and it stayed `True` even at 10,000 rows. Average precision rewards a model's *ranking variation*; a baseline predicting one tied value for the whole population (a single segment) has zero ranking power and is close to unbeatable-proof under PR-AUC regardless of true signal — a real property of the metric, not a bug. Giving `is_bot_author` genuine variety and asserting `roc_auc` near 0.5 instead (the metric that actually answers "was a real relationship learned") is the honest, robust test; forcing a specific seed/`n` to make `beats_baseline is False` pass would have been closer to p-hacking the test than testing the gate. `mypy` needed one narrowing (`np.asarray(...)` on `predict_proba`'s output before `[:, 1]`) for the same lightgbm-return-type reason Task 7's `assert isinstance(runs, pd.DataFrame)` existed. |

| 2026-09-05 | Phase 5 Task 7 — the partition fix held; the re-run then hit a fork-time worker stall | Post-mortem of real run `630533628470951` (cancelled after 90 min execution): delivered driver `log4j`/`stacktrace.log` + executor `stderr` from the cluster-log volume; live web search on `TOKENIZERS_PARALLELISM` / `predict_batch_udf`; `pytest tests/unit/test_embed_pipeline.py` + `test_embed_pipeline_cli.py` (2 new); full suite | **The `repartition(16)` fix worked** — the encode stage (`ResultStage 7`) was submitted with exactly 16 tasks, one per slot, not 967. **Then every task froze.** `HangingTaskDetector`: `shuffle.read.recordsRead` stuck at 28,909 for task 14 from 14:03:55 on, `output.recordsWritten -> 0`; `MapInBatchEvaluator: Idle timeout ... No data received from the worker process ... isAlive = Some(true)`; task threads `RUNNABLE` blocked reading the Python pipe. A frozen read counter with a live silent worker is a **Python-worker stall**, not slow compute (28,909 texts is ~3 min of encode at Task 1's 151 texts/s; the worker produced nothing for 10-50×). **Proven: n=1, a worker-side stall. Provisional: the exact mechanism** — the signature matches the documented `tokenizers` (Rayon) / `torch` (OpenMP) threadpool-across-fork deadlock, but the one log that would name it (the per-task worker stderr) is node-local and died with the cluster. **Fix**: job cluster sets `TOKENIZERS_PARALLELISM=false` + `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1` (`OMP=1` is right on merit too — 16 single-thread workers on 16 vCPUs); `_embed_partition` sets both defensively and prints per-batch progress to stderr so the next run's logs are conclusive; new `--limit` / `embeddings_limit` for a bounded (~200K-text, <$1) proof run before the full corpus. `predict_batch_udf` (the current Spark-4.0 API for this, which would also drop the `repartition` shuffle) noted as the better path, deferred — the blocker is orthogonal to the UDF API and a second same-function rewrite mid-phase under a finite credit carries its own risk. `docs/findings/2026-09-05-embedding-worker-fork-deadlock.md`. ≈$3.85 this run, ≈$15 of trial credit spent reaching a first real embedding run, still no table. | 2026-09-05 | Phase 5 Task 7 — the fork fix held end-to-end; the index is scoped to recent events | Proof run `572931281110752` (`--limit 200000`) to TERMINATED/SUCCESS; `az vm list-usage --location westus3` for GPU quota; per-day qualifying-text counts against real Bronze; `pytest tests/unit/test_embed_pipeline.py`/`test_embed_pipeline_cli.py` (2 new); full suite | **The tokenizers/OpenMP fork fix works.** The proof run's encode (`ResultStage 10`) ran exactly 16 tasks, all 16 completed (`finished in 1,192,187 ms`), `[embed]` progress flowing, zero freeze — 83.9 min, ≈$3.31, 400 K rows written + registered. **Throughput measured: ~168 texts/s aggregate** (~10.5/s per vCPU), so the full 14,924,573-text corpus is a **~25 h** CPU-bound encode — the old "~1.7 h" figure took Task 1's 151 texts/s as a per-core rate and ×16, a Gate-1 error now noted in the findings doc. **GPU is quota-blocked**: `az vm list-usage` shows every GPU VM family at 0/0 in `westus3`. CPU headroom is ~2.7× (Ddsv6 20→48 vCPU) → ~9 h / ≈$35. **Decision**: build the index over **recent events** — new `--since-date` / `embeddings_since` (Bronze `event_date` partition prune), `2025-09-20` measured at **~1.73 M** qualifying texts (~2.9 h, ≈$7). Every Phase 5 capability is demonstrated identically at this size; the full-corpus build is a one-parameter change plus a bigger cluster, the same shape as the roadmap's other deliberately-not-built extension points. `docs/findings/2026-09-05-embedding-worker-fork-deadlock.md`. **Scoped run `603111972146916`: TERMINATED/SUCCESS, 135.8 min, ≈$5.36, 1,459,551 new texts** — the fork fix held the whole run, both passes, no freeze. `almanac_dbx.embeddings.pr_issue_embeddings` now 1,859,551 rows (1.46 M scoped + the proof run's 0.4 M). ≈$24 cumulative embeddings spend. |
| 2026-09-06 | Phase 5 Tasks 4/6 — the real similarity run, and three bugs only real data could surface | Four real `databricks_job.pr_similarity` runs (`705047433065245` crashed, `647656767930289` and `26796189966426` inert, `1097688475035466` correct) + `databricks_job.similarity_comparison` run `754385014066975`; a live 6-variant filter probe and a 25-row neighbour probe against the deployed index; four read-only population measurements against Gold/Silver/the embeddings table; MLflow metrics and the UC registry read back directly; full suite `uv run pytest -q` (**326 passed, 0 failed, 1485.20s**) | **Three bugs, each hiding the next, none visible to `ruff`/`mypy`/the local suite.** (1) **Every issue embedding got a degenerate `issue:<repo_id>` key** — `extract_texts` read `$.payload.number`, correct for a PullRequestEvent but wrong for an IssuesEvent (`$.payload.issue.number`), and `concat_ws` silently drops the null: 718,086 of 1,859,551 rows (39%), **594,181 issue vectors lost to the collapse**, zero `pr:` keys affected. Fixed forward; `_parse_entity_key` also made total (returns `None`, never raises) so a malformed key counts but never resolves. The deployed index keeps the degenerate keys — recovery is a **re-key in place, not a re-embed** (Bronze join on `(text_hash, event_time, repo_id)` resolves 717,353, 99.90%), ≈$5 and ~2 h of encode saved. (2) **The spine reached back before the embedded window** — sampled the whole quarter against an index holding only `>= 2025-09-20`, and the point-in-time filter only ever returns *older* neighbours; fixed with `--since-date` wired to the same `var.embeddings_since`. (3) **The spine was read twice and `rand` is nondeterministic** — `compute_pr_similarity` collected the spine for its queries, then re-scanned it for the final join, so ~62,000 real neighbour pairs computed against sample A were joined against sample B, leaving **44 of 10,000 rows** (0.44%). Diagnosed by elimination, not by guessing the next plausible component: the join was measured healthy (62%), all six `event_time` filter encodings probed live and working, and 25/25 real spine rows returned a full k=10 — so `pairs` had to be populated, which the output itself confirmed (the empty-`pairs` branch nulls *every* rate; 30 were non-null). Expected coincidental overlap of two independent 10,000-row samples from 1,564,185 rows × the 62% embedding rate predicts **~40**; observed **44**. **Not reproduced locally** (n=1, simple `range` source — deterministic), so the conclusion is labelled as resting on elimination. Fixed by collecting once and building the join's keys from that same list; pinned by a test that *counts reads*, since a second evaluation is invisible whenever the samples agree. **Task 4's real output** (`1097688475035466`, 321 s + 840 s): 6,210 of 10,000 rows with neighbours = **62.10%**, matching the independently pre-measured 62% join rate to two decimals, `avg(similar_neighbor_count) = 6.21 = 0.621 × 10`, 4,785 non-null rates averaging 0.2657 — a partially-working run would not land on the predicted number. **Two recorded numbers corrected**: the "~34 ms/query from the cluster" figure divided 342 s by 10,000 spine rows when most issued no query at all (honest figure **~135 ms**, from 840 s ÷ ~6,210 real queries), and a first pass at Task 6's trainable population said 8.9% using the wrong predicate (`compute_breach_labels` also trains on `closed_no_response`; the real figure is **48.6%**, 760,715 of 1,564,165) — at 8.9% the fix would have been a ~2.7 h, ≈$6.50 job that was never necessary. **Task 6's result is neither the clean win nor the clean null §8.3a anticipated**: holding the candidate fixed, similarity is worth **+0.0484 AP (0.4848 → 0.5332, +10.0%)**, with ROC-AUC 0.8011 → 0.8198 and log-loss 0.3977 → 0.3791 moving the same way; best-of-arm is +0.0379 but mixes in a candidate switch, and `is_unbalance` got slightly *worse* (−0.0063). The identical `baseline` AP of **0.2172 in both arms** is the check that the comparison is genuinely paired. **`beats_champion = False`** (0.5332 < 0.612) and the registry confirms the gate fired rather than merely being computed — one version, `@champion` still on version 1, despite `--register` — so `train_model` does **not** get the similarity parameters wired in. Two caveats stated with the number: the 0.612 champion was measured on the full population and this on ~4,900 sampled rows, and the PR-dense index (bug 1) *favours* the similarity features, making +0.0484 an upper estimate rather than a floor. `docs/findings/2026-09-06-similarity-real-run.md`. |
| 2026-09-06 | Phase 5 Task 8 — wrap-up and exit gate | Every gate row walked against a real artifact, not a plan: Task 1's findings doc, Task 2/3/4's tests, the live `similar_prs` demo against `almanac-embeddings`, Task 6's two MLflow experiments and the UC registry; README Mermaid `V` node moved to `done` in this commit per the same-commit rule; full suite `uv run pytest -q` (326 passed, 0 failed, 1485.20s) | **Phase 5 complete — 8 of 8.** Exit gate, row by row: the index-technology decision came from a **real-measured 14,924,573-text corpus** (~3.2× §8.3's stale 5%-sample projection) rather than the stale number, with the SKU rate recorded as an honest open gap when `system.billing.list_prices` returned nothing. **Point-in-time filtering holds on both axes** — query-side candidacy is passed into the index's own filter (all six `event_time` encodings probed live) and label-side knowability keeps an unresolved neighbour null rather than "no breach"; the live `similar_prs` demo asserted every returned neighbour was strictly older than its query's `as_of`, on real data. **No brute-force path exists**: every real query goes through the ANN index, never a Spark self-join over the corpus. **The pipeline is incremental** (`left_anti` on `text_hash`), proven by the scoped run embedding 1,459,551 of ~1.73 M qualifying texts and skipping the proof run's overlap. **`POST /similar-prs` is demoed against the real live index** — three real PRs, k=5, and the first sampled PR's nearest neighbour is the immediately preceding PR in its own repo (`pr:998922291:1476` → `pr:998922291:1475`, 21 minutes earlier), with an unknown PR returning `[]`; the index/endpoint/vectors/query path are all real, the 3-row `embeddings` frame passed in is not the full Delta table, which is stated rather than glossed. **The champion gate held under its own terms**: `beats_champion = False`, and the registry read back one version with `@champion` still on version 1 despite `--register`, so nothing was promoted and `train_model` stays unwired. **Measured lift, honestly bounded** — +0.0484 AP like-for-like (+10.0%), ROC-AUC and log-loss agreeing, but on ~4,900 sampled rows against a champion measured on the full population, and on an index whose collapsed issue keys *favour* the similarity features. Neither the clean win nor the clean null the phase table anticipated, and recorded as exactly that. **Real spend this phase ≈$31** (≈$24 embeddings across two cancelled runs, a proof run and the scoped run; ≈$2.4 across four `pr_similarity` runs; ≈$0.6 the comparison), against the $184 credit expiring 2026-09-24. Deferred and unchanged from §8.3a: hybrid/lexical search and reranking, other embedding models, a standalone issue-dedup surface, GPU inference, the `@challenger` workflow, `GET /features/{id}?as_of=` as a service. One follow-up is named rather than silently carried: the 718,086 degenerate `issue:<repo_id>` embeddings are re-keyable in place via a Bronze join (99.90%) plus an index resync, ≈$5 and ~2 h cheaper than re-embedding. |
| 2026-09-06 | Vector Search's real idle cost, measured before teardown | `system.billing.usage` at hourly granularity joined to `system.billing.list_prices`, attributed per endpoint via `usage_metadata.endpoint_name`; endpoint/index metadata and a live `similar_prs` query captured before any destructive step | **A `STANDARD` Vector Search endpoint does not scale to zero, and the same billing SKU hides two opposite behaviours.** `almanac-embeddings` drew a flat **4.000 DBU/hour, every hour** — identical in the window where ~12,400 real queries ran and in the windows where nothing touched it. The Phase 4 model-serving endpoint bills under the *same* SKU (`PREMIUM_SERVERLESS_REAL_TIME_INFERENCE_US_WEST_3`) and drew DBUs only during its 2026-09-04 live window, then zero — so "serverless" says nothing about idle cost here, and the two must be reasoned about separately. Measured rate **96 DBU/day ≈ $6.72/day ≈ $202/month idle**, against $65.75 of total Databricks spend to date and a $184 credit expiring 2026-09-24: standing idle to expiry would have cost ≈$121, and to Phase 7's demo window (§9, Nov 9–22 — the next actual use, since Phase 6 needs the online store rather than this index) ≈$430. There is no measurable per-query charge; the hourly rate is the whole cost, which makes a bounded heavily-used window efficient and a long idle one the worst case. **Everything perishable captured first** in `docs/findings/2026-09-06-vector-search-live-state-and-teardown.md` — endpoint and index metadata (`indexed_row_count` 1,265,363, dim 384, DELTA_SYNC/TRIGGERED/HYBRID), and a live k=5 `similar_prs` trace byte-identical to one taken ~10 h earlier. Teardown destroys only the endpoint and its synced ANN copy: the 1,859,551-row embeddings Delta table (≈$24 of encode), both MLflow experiments, the registered champion and the 341M-row quarter all survive, so recreation is `terraform apply` plus one sync, never a re-embed. Noted with it: `vector_search.tf`'s `prevent_destroy` guard exists for a *different* failure (provider drift recreating the index on a plain apply) and must be restored after any deliberate teardown. |
| 2026-09-06 | Vector Search torn down; identity scrubbed from history | `delete-index`/`delete-endpoint` + `terraform state rm`, verified by `list-endpoints`, `terraform plan` and `jobs list-runs`; `git filter-repo --mailmap` over 126 commits, verified by re-reading identities off `origin/main` after a `--force-with-lease` push | **Teardown done, and deliberately not via `terraform destroy`.** A targeted destroy planned **3 resources, not 2** — `databricks_job.pr_similarity` followed as a dependent because its parameters reference the endpoint and index names, and deleting a Databricks job deletes its run history, which is the evidence for four real runs including the crash that found bug 1 and the run that proved bug 3 fixed. The job costs nothing idle, so the cascade bought nothing; Terraform 1.15.8 has no `-exclude` usable with `-target`, so the two resources were deleted directly and state reconciled with `state rm`. Verified after: `list-endpoints` empty, `terraform plan` **2 to add, 0 to change, 0 to destroy**, all four runs still listed. `prevent_destroy` on the index is off for the teardown with a comment saying to restore it when recreating, since it guards the unrelated provider-drift failure. **Also confirmed the endpoint was the only thing burning**: 0 non-terminated clusters, SQL warehouse stopped on a 10-minute auto-stop. **Identity scrub, separately**: history carried three identities, and the one that mattered was not the email — the committer identity leaked a local username **and a machine hostname containing a personal name** (both masked here on 2026-09-08, by the Task 8 check — a postmortem that quotes the identifier it is removing republishes it), and no amount of file scrubbing would have caught it. All three collapsed to the GitHub `noreply` address via `filter-repo`; **the address form matters** — GitHub's reference doc splits on 2017-07-18 and this account (created 2026-05-12) needs `ID+username@`, not the bare `username@` form, or commits stop being attributed. The rewrite invalidated 16 commit SHAs cited across the docs, which were remapped from `filter-repo`'s own commit-map rather than by hand and spot-checked by resolving the new SHAs back to their subjects. GitHub primary-email visibility also set to private. `docs/findings/2026-09-06-vector-search-live-state-and-teardown.md`. |
| 2026-09-06 | Console evidence curated, PII-scrubbed, committed | Reviewed all 20 screenshots from the working set individually; masked with PIL and re-read each masked image to confirm the region landed; cross-checked every number in frame against the findings doc that already recorded it | **Nine kept of twenty**, in `docs/images/`, gathered in `docs/findings/2026-09-06-console-evidence.md` — a second, independent surface for numbers already measured, which matters more than usual because the Vector Search infrastructure behind some of these no longer exists to re-capture. The load-bearing frames: the backfill run showing **`days_processed: 92`, `rows_bronze: 341,060,851`, `hours_missing: []` under a `Failed` header with `SystemExit: 0`** — the ninth defect made visible in one frame; six consecutive `RunExecutionError` backfill runs (one burning 5h 2m 51s) behind STATUS's "completed on the seventh trigger"; the Photon A/B decided at **13m 41s vs 18m 8s** on identical hardware; Unity Catalog's own lineage view (5 upstream, 5 downstream); the four ADLS medallion containers; and Phase 5's run sequence including both `UserCanceled` embedding runs (4h 49m 40s, 1h 37m 35s) that priced the partition-reload and fork-deadlock defects. **Five images needed masking** — `Run as`/`Created by` columns, one account-header strip, one workspace host — and each mask was verified by re-reading the image, not assumed from coordinates. **Two files were rejected outright and nothing from them entered the repo**: they were not Almanac at all but a staffing-portal timesheet carrying a third party's full name, a real employer name, and a **live `authenticationKey` in the URL** — a reminder that a folder named for a project is not evidence that everything in it belongs to that project, and that reviewing each file individually is the check that caught it. Eleven others were duplicate views; where two frames showed the same thing the finished-state one was kept. |
| 2026-09-06 | CI concurrency — stop redundant runs racing each other | Observed six simultaneous `in_progress` CI runs on `main` from a burst of docs-only commits; cancelled five, then added a `concurrency` group; YAML re-parsed to confirm the block is well-formed | **Six full suites were running at once, five of them already superseded.** The workflow triggers on every push to `main` with no concurrency control and no path filtering, and the suite takes ~45 min on a 2-core runner, so a batch of commits leaves a queue of runs all testing older trees than HEAD — only the newest result means anything. Fixed with `concurrency: {group: ci-${{ github.ref }}, cancel-in-progress: true}`: one run per ref, a newer push cancels the older. `github.ref` in a group name is not a shell interpolation and is a ref rather than user-authored text, so it carries none of the workflow-injection risk the same expression would inside a `run:` step. **Deliberately not added: `paths-ignore` for docs-only pushes.** It would have skipped every commit in this session, but the repo's own testing policy is that the full suite runs before every push, and a `docs/**` filter silently stops enforcing that the moment a commit touches both docs and code — the failure mode is a green tick that ran nothing. Concurrency solves the actual complaint (wasted parallel runs) without weakening the gate. **⚠️ Corrected later the same day — the reasoning in this last sentence is wrong; `paths-ignore` was added. See the next row.** |
| 2026-09-06 | CI path filtering — skip docs-only pushes (and a correction to the row above) | Read the Actions workflow-syntax reference for `paths-ignore` semantics; `git ls-files docs/` to confirm docs are inert; re-parsed the YAML; replayed the filter against three real commits from this session's own history | **The previous row's stated reason for refusing `paths-ignore` was wrong, and this row corrects it.** That reason was "a `docs/**` filter stops enforcing the full suite the moment a commit touches both docs and code." GitHub does not work that way: **"When all the path names match patterns in `paths-ignore`, the workflow will not run"** (workflow-syntax reference, checked 2026-09-06) — *all*, not *any*, so a mixed docs+code push still runs the whole suite. Demonstrated rather than asserted, by replaying the filter over this session's real commits: `256517a` (`ci.yml` **and** `docs/STATUS.md`, the exact mixed case the objection was about) → **runs**; the two docs-only commits → **skip**. Also verified docs are genuinely inert to CI: `docs/` is only `.md` and `.png`, and the `docs/findings/...` strings inside `src/` are prose citations in comments, not file reads. Filter is `docs/**`, `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` — narrow on purpose, not `**.md`, so a future `.md` that CI *does* read cannot be silently excluded. **Applied to `push` only, not `pull_request`:** a skipped run reports no status at all, so a filtered docs-only PR would hang forever on a required status check. Branch protection is unavailable today (the API returns 403 for a private repo on the free plan) but becomes available the moment this repo goes public, and every one of the six redundant runs that prompted this was a push to `main` — so filtering `push` alone fixes the real complaint without arming that trap. **Process note:** the original claim was recorded as settled without checking the primary source, which is exactly the gate-1/gate-2 failure the design-decision skill exists to catch — the check that overturned it took under a minute. |
| 2026-09-06 | Phase 6 — streaming + online store concretized, before any code | Two live probes of `api.github.com/events` (headers + payload shape); live Gate 2 check of Databricks' online-store API against Microsoft Learn's own page (updated 2026-08-28); `grep` of `features/registration.py` and `embed/pipeline.py` for the documented `publish_table` prerequisites; §9's phase table re-read against the new section | **Recorded in a new §4.6 plus the 8-task plan (`docs/plans/2026-09-06-phase-6-streaming-plan.md`), no code yet.** **The live Events API is in the reduced era too** — one poll, n=29 `PullRequestEvent`s, `payload.pull_request` of exactly **5 keys**, with `merged`/`draft`/`user`/`title`/`body`/`additions` all absent — confirming §4.5a's "only the Events firehose was reduced" from the live side, and constraining the phase structurally: **live events cannot produce §5.1's label or any text feature**, so "online feature freshness" means the features the reduced payload supports, and re-serving the Phase 4 champion on live data is named impossible rather than merely unbuilt. **It is also a lossy tail, not a firehose**: `x-poll-interval: 60`, `Link` terminating at `page=3` (~300 events retrievable), against §12's measured ~155–162K events/hour — on the order of **11% capture**. Two consequences: authentication is mandatory arithmetic, not an optimization (3 pages × 60 polls/hr = 180 requests against an unauthenticated ceiling of 60), and **§11's replay harness becomes load-bearing** — the live feed structurally cannot demonstrate completeness, late arrival or duplication, so replay of real archive hours through the identical path is the only way to force those cases. **Both probes are `n=1` and are labelled as such in the design doc**; Task 1 widens them before anything is provisioned, per Gate 1. **Gate 2 changed the answer on the online store**: Databricks **legacy online tables are deprecated and inaccessible after 2026-01-15** — already past — so the target is a **Lakebase Autoscaling** project via `fe.create_online_store`/`fe.publish_table` (`databricks-feature-engineering>=0.13.0`); designing from training data would have produced a dead architecture. **The cost trap repeats and is caught before the spend this time**: Databricks documents that **"Lakebase scale-to-zero is not supported"**, the same shape `2026-09-06-vector-search-live-state-and-teardown.md` measured the hard way at a flat 4 DBU/hour, so Task 7 ships the teardown path in the same change that creates the store rather than correcting afterwards. **Two prerequisite gaps found by checking rather than assuming**: feature tables lack `delta.enableChangeDataFeed` (set only in `embed/pipeline.py`) and their PK columns are not `NOT NULL`, both required by `publish_table` — while the `TIMESERIES` primary key §4.4a added "for governance/lineage only, not correctness" turns out to be exactly the prerequisite it demands. **Two documentation defects fixed in the same commit**: §9's phase table still read "Nov 2–8" against §4.6's pulled-forward window (the identical two-sections-of-one-document contradiction recorded on 2026-09-01), and **STATUS.md's own `## Current position` still described Phase 2 as current** — unrevised through Phases 3, 4 and 5, the exact drift this file's header rule exists to prevent. |
| 2026-09-06 | Phase 6 Task 1 — Events API capture, real cloud spend, and the online-store rate | 30 authenticated polls of `api.github.com/events` at the advertised `x-poll-interval` (`scripts/measure_events_api.py`, committed so the finding is re-runnable); Azure Cost Management API (`ActualCost`, daily, by `ServiceName`) cross-checked against `system.billing.usage` ⋈ `list_prices`; Azure Retail Prices API for the SKU rates Databricks' own catalog omits | **Green, Task 1 complete — and two of the four numbers moved materially.** **Capture is ~7.1–7.4%, not §4.6's ~11%** (`n=30` vs `n=1`): **zero event-id overlap between consecutive polls on all 30**, mean 192.2 events/window, ~11,500/hour against §12's archive-measured ~155–162K/hour. The window turns over completely inside 60s, so the live feed is a *sample*, not a firehose — which promotes §11's replay harness from nice-to-have to the only thing that can prove completeness. **The rate limit is not the binding constraint**: ~180 requests/hour used of 5,000, so the 7% ceiling comes from honouring `x-poll-interval`, a courtesy header, not a technical limit — recorded as an explicit decision (honour it) rather than left implicit, because it *is* the cause of the 7%. **A conditional request never returned 304 in 5 attempts** — the feed moves faster than two back-to-back requests — so the planned "is a 304 rate-limit-exempt?" question could not be reached, and the real answer is that conditional requests are not a poll-budget optimisation here. **A stable 305s feed lag** is a floor on end-to-end freshness that no pipeline work can beat; Task 9 must report freshness decomposed or it will read as pipeline latency when it is mostly GitHub's. **The cost finding has the widest blast radius: Databricks DBUs are only 53% of real Azure spend** — $65.19 of $122.40 across 09-01→09-06, with VMs ($30.65), NAT Gateway ($14.97) and Storage ($11.05) appearing in no prior findings doc — so remaining credit was **~$61.60, not the ~$118** a DBU-only reading implies, and *every* earlier cost figure in this repo understates real spend by ~47%. **A first-pass "standing cost" of $4.43/day was my own error and is corrected in place**: NAT bills per-GB and Storage per-transaction, so both track activity rather than sitting flat (NAT swings $6.33→$0.52 across days); the true idle rate is ~$2.30/day. **The Lakebase rate remains unresolved and is named as such** — `list_prices` returns zero rows for `LAKEBASE`/`POSTGRES`/`ONLINE`/`OLTP`, the *second* occurrence of the gap Phase 5 hit for `VECTOR`/`SEARCH`, so this is a pattern rather than a one-off; Azure's Retail Prices API lists "Premium Database Serverless Compute" at $0.26/DBU-hour in `westus3` as the **probable but unconfirmed** meter, to be confirmed the way Vector Search's was, by provisioning it. **That catalog cross-validated the Phase 5 measurement exactly**: 4 DBU/h × 24 × $0.07 = **$6.72/day**, matching `2026-09-06-vector-search-live-state-and-teardown.md` to the cent, which is what justifies using the Retail API for rates the Databricks catalog omits. **One measurement of my own was built, found unusable, and removed rather than shipped**: a `created_at` window-span metric intended to derive capture without a second data source, defeated by a minority of genuinely old events (median age 302s, max 194,271s ≈ 2.2 days) that blew out even a p5–p95 robust span; the committed script omits it. Also recorded: **§11 gains "credit expiry is a budget, not a wall" and "teardown ships with provisioning"** at the user's direction, and §4.6's superseded ~11% is marked in place rather than overwritten. `docs/findings/2026-09-06-events-api-and-online-store-rates.md`. |
| 2026-09-06 | Phase 6 Task 2 — event-stream config and the poller | `pytest tests/unit/test_stream_poller.py tests/unit/test_rest.py -v` (16 new tests, 6 pre-existing REST tests untouched and green after the `_next_link`→`next_link` rename); whole-repo `make check` (`ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, `mypy src tests`, `pytest -m "not network"`) — 341 passed, 0 failed, 0 errors, 26m59s | **Green, Task 2 complete.** New `EventStreamConfig` in `pipeline/source.py` — a third source shape alongside `SourceConfig`/`RestSourceConfig`, one fixed URL rather than a per-entity template, composing the existing `AuthConfig`/`RateLimitConfig` rather than redefining them. New `almanac.stream.poller`: `poll_once` follows pagination up to the measured `pages_per_poll=3` ceiling, dedups events by id across pages, sends `If-None-Match` and treats `304` as no-new-events rather than an error, and backs off on `429` unconditionally but on `403` only when the budget is actually exhausted (GitHub returns 403 for real auth failures too) — an explicit, tested improvement over `extract.rest`'s existing all-`(403,429)`-alike backoff, made because Task 1 measured that a live `429` needs handling, not a raised limit. **The token is never a literal anywhere**: `resolve_token` reads `AuthConfig.token_env` from the environment, and a dedicated test asserts a forced HTTP error's message never contains the token value. **One real bug caught before shipping, not by the plan's own test list**: `_write_poll`'s original filename was timestamp-only, and `Path.replace` silently overwrites — under a fixed clock (a test, or Task 4's replay harness, which needs deterministic timestamps) two non-empty polls would collapse into one file with no signal, the same *silent data loss* class as Phase 5's Bug 3. Fixed by folding the poll index into the filename; a new regression test drives two same-timestamp non-empty polls through `run_poller` and asserts both files survive. **One shared function promoted rather than duplicated**: `extract.rest`'s private `_next_link` (RFC 8288 `Link`-header parsing) is renamed to public `next_link` and imported by the poller — pure, config-independent, and the two REST-derived sources would otherwise carry two copies of the identical fact, exactly the drift pattern this project's own `RAW_SCHEMA`/`raw()` precedent exists to avoid; the six pre-existing `test_rest.py` tests confirm the rename changed no behavior. **One real mypy-strict gap surfaced and fixed**: a `for page in range(...)` loop with an internal `break` left `url: str | None` unnarrowed at the top of the next iteration; restructured to a `while url is not None and page < pages_per_poll` loop, which both type-narrows correctly and states the actual termination condition more directly. **Two deviations from the plan's own sketch**, both toward the codebase's real conventions rather than its guess: config lives at `conf/sources/github_events.yml` (matching `github_rest.yml`), not `config/sources/*.yaml`; and `poll_once`/`run_poller` take a composed `PollerSession` (mirroring `extract.rest.RestSession`'s exact shape) rather than the plan's sketched bare `RestClient` parameter, which does not exist in this codebase. |

Fixed with Spark's `observe()` API, attached *before* the watermark step in the query plan, read back via `query.recentProgress`'s `observedMetrics` — the only point in the pipeline that sees a row Spark is about to drop. **`write_stream_silver` gained an `available_now: bool = False` parameter** after a caught-before-running bug: the first draft had no `trigger()` call at all, which defaults to Spark's continuous micro-batch trigger and would have hung every test until `run_streaming_query`'s own bounded `awaitTermination` timeout turned it into a failure; `available_now=True` gives tests (and Task 4's replay harness) a bounded, self-terminating run while production Task 9 keeps the continuous default. **Idempotent-write correctness proven across a real restart, not asserted**: `write_stream_silver` sets `txnAppId`/`txnVersion` in `foreachBatch` per Delta's documented idempotent-streaming-write contract (checked live against `docs.delta.io` — `txnAppId` must stay stable across restarts of the *same* checkpoint or duplicate-write protection silently breaks); `test_exactly_once_across_a_restart_from_checkpoint` stops a query mid-stream, restarts a brand-new `StreamingQuery` object against the same checkpoint, and proves the result is byte-identical to one uninterrupted run over the same data, while a second test proves a restart with nothing new to process re-appends nothing. **A real test-design bug was caught and fixed, not just noticed**: the first version of that test gave the "interrupted" and "reference" runs different `polled_at` poll-file boundaries, so `ingested_at` legitimately differed between the two tables for a reason unrelated to exactly-once; diagnosed by reading the actual pytest failure diff (itself requiring a re-run after a `tail`-piped background capture truncated the real traceback under log noise) and fixed by giving both runs identical poll-file structure and `polled_at` values, varying only the interruption. | 2026-09-06 | Test-suite speed: xdist + shuffle-partition default + a fast inner-loop target | `make check` before and after, same 364 tests, green both times | **`make check` 34m38s → 15m12s (2.3×), and `make check-fast` is 25s.** Three changes, no test weakened: (1) `spark.py`'s shared builder now sets `spark.sql.shuffle.partitions=8` (from Spark's default 200) and `spark.ui.enabled=false` — this session only ever runs `local[*]` against ≤ a few thousand rows, so 200 near-empty tasks per join/window/aggregation across a suite of thousands was pure scheduler overhead; Databricks sets its own from the cluster (a job task takes `active_or_local_session`'s existing session, never this builder) and `embed.pipeline`'s one partition-count-sensitive path passes an explicit `repartition()`, so neither is touched. (2) `pytest-xdist`, `make test` → `-n 4` — four workers, each with its own session-scoped `SparkSession`, tuned for a local 8-core/16 GB machine; **no test proved xdist-unsafe** (364/364 green on the first parallel run). (3) new `make check-fast` / `make test-fast` — lint + mypy + only the 195 non-`spark` tests, the inner-loop counterpart to the full gate, explicitly **not** a substitute for `make check` before a push. CI keeps the plain serial `pytest` invocation in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (2-core runner, no room for `-n 4`) but picks up the shuffle-partition win for free. |
