| 2026-09-10 | Phase 9 Task 9 -- the model gateway, in four parts | TDD with **RED watched** (`ModuleNotFoundError`, no `almanac.agent.model_gateway`); **mutation-tested twelve ways**, all twelve killed against an explicitly recorded green 12-test baseline; `make check-fast` -- lint, `mypy --strict`, **395 passed** | **All three of design doc S9.3's live findings, fixed and tested without spending anything** -- every assertion is on a request or a stub, so the whole module is free. **9a, capability records, default-deny.** `SAMPLING_SUPPORT` carries one entry: the primary, checked and accepting **none** of `temperature`, `top_p`, `top_k`. An empty set there is a different claim from absence, and an endpoint with no entry refuses every sampling parameter with a message saying it *has not been checked* -- `LAKEBASE_REGIONS`' rule, learned from a region nobody checked that cost 44 minutes. It **refuses rather than strips**, because a silently dropped `temperature` leaves the caller believing it applied. **The body test is on the actual wire body**, captured through an `httpx2.MockTransport`, and that choice paid immediately: `pydantic-ai` sends `max_tokens` as **`max_completion_tokens`**, so a test asserting on the settings dict would have proved nothing about what the endpoint receives. **9b, the fallback policy, is the finding that matters.** `FallbackModel` defaults to falling back on any `ModelAPIError`, which includes 4xx, so one stray `temperature` would 400 every primary call, the fallback would answer, the run would succeed, and Phase 10 would score a model nobody chose. `fallback_on` is an explicit predicate instead. **A probe pinned how the four shapes actually arrive** rather than assuming: an `APIStatusError` becomes a `ModelHTTPError` carrying the status code, while an `APIConnectionError` -- which `APITimeoutError` subclasses -- becomes a bare `ModelAPIError` with none, so "connection-level" is a type test and not a status test. Four shapes are tested, three transient and one not: a 400 raises and the fallback handler is asserted **never reached**. One trap worth recording: the predicate's parameter must be annotated `Exception`, because `pydantic-ai` tells an exception handler from a response handler by that annotation alone. **9c, which model answered, is read back from `ModelResponse.model_name`** -- the server's own statement -- and lands in Task 8's audit record, which gains a `model` field; with a fallback in the chain the configured primary is a guess, and Phase 8 already found a guess exactly like that wrong on the serving endpoint. **9d, preflight**, asks the workspace rather than the docs, since the general-purpose table omits `databricks-claude-sonnet-5` while the endpoint reports READY here; it refuses a missing or `NOT_READY` endpoint with a message naming the symptom (a 404 on the first call **inside** a paid window) and what *is* serving, and its tests use the real SDK `ServingEndpoint` types rather than a hand-rolled stub. `Workspace` groups the connection facts so the primary and fallback differ in their name and nothing else, which is what "one seam" has to mean. **`openai` and `httpx2` are now declared** in the `agent` extra: both arrived transitively and both are imported directly here, which is the transitive-dependency trap a resolver is free to spring. Not pushed: the full `make check` has not run since this change. |
| 2026-09-10 | Phase 9 Task 10 -- the bounded agent, and the replay harness | TDD with **RED watched** (`ModuleNotFoundError`, no `almanac.agent.bounded_agent`); **mutation-tested ten ways**, all ten killed against an explicitly recorded green 8-test baseline; `make check-fast` -- lint, `mypy --strict`, **395 passed**. **Nothing was spent**: the loop, the gateway, the tools and the Delta tables under them are all real, and the only stub is the thing that would cost money | **The agent's tools are derived from the served surface, not restated.** `Tool.from_schema` takes each tool's name, description and JSON schema from what the MCP server advertises -- which came from Task 2's models -- so a hand-written fourth copy of the tool list, the one that would go stale silently, does not exist. Every call routes through Task 8's gateway, so a tool the gateway disallows is **never offered to the model at all** rather than offered and then refused. **The turn bound is a request limit and is tested where it bites**: one tool call plus an answer costs exactly two model requests, so a limit of two answers and a limit of one stops. Hitting it returns a structured `Incomplete` carrying the reason, the tools already called and the transcript -- never a truncated answer, which reads like a finished one. **The replay harness found a real defect in itself.** `FunctionModel` stamps its own name on every response it returns, so a naive replay reported `function:next_response:` as the model that answered; every replayed run in Phase 10's suite would have been attributable to nothing. The name of the response that *ended* the recorded run is carried over instead, because that is the one `answering_model` reads, and a mutation removing it turns the round-trip test red. **`Strict` is now exported from `schemas.py`** rather than private: the audit record and the two agent outcomes were the second and third users of the same `extra="forbid", frozen=True` base, which is CLAUDE.md item 4's threshold rather than its counter-case. The instructions state the governing rule to the model as well as enforcing it around one: every number must come from a tool call in this run, and a refusal is reported as a refusal with no number in its place. **What is not done: the transcripts have not been captured live.** The plan captures them on the first real run, and that run belongs inside Task 11's window rather than ahead of it. Not pushed: the full `make check` has not run since this change. |
| 2026-09-10 | Phase 9 Task 11 -- the window: four tools on real data, one live answer, and an audit record that timed the wrong thing | Five one-time `jobs submit` runs on self-terminating job clusters under `almanac-paid-window`'s five gates, each wheel **sha256-verified identical** on both sides; evidence read back from the UC volume and scanned by the pseudonymity guard; teardown verified twice after the last run, at 14:24:47Z and 14:29:07Z; the per-request audit fix TDD'd with **RED watched** -- three behavioural failures, not import errors -- and **mutation-tested six ways**, all six killed against a green 18-test baseline; `make check-fast` -- lint, `mypy --strict`, **411 passed**; every agent test module, Spark included, **100 passed** | **The tools hold on real data, and the agent answered -- through a substitute.** Against `events/clean` v92 and the feature tables at v0, `versions()` matched the live registry (champion v2, run `5f6a71bd9e4f4b6d8f7ea0a6454c0ca5`), point-in-time held at two instants on one real PR with a **byte-identical** recompute, and both scoring tools refused a real 2026 entity rather than scoring it. **The refusal's cause is not the one design doc S4.2 predicted**: it named `bot_events_to_date`, because the repo was first seen in 2026 and the feature tables cover Q3 2025 -- so it proves the mechanism on coverage, not on the `is_draft` drift, `n = 1`. **Neither configured model can serve the agent here.** Every Claude endpoint returns 403 for a Databricks-set rate limit of 0 while reporting `READY`, and `gpt-oss-120b` rejects `max_completion_tokens` and returns a reply `OpenAIChatModel` cannot parse. **The fallback policy held on the 403 exactly as Task 9 built it**: permanent, so the fallback was never consulted. Llama 3.3 70B answered as a substitute chosen with the user and recorded as one (`substitute_for` in the evidence), calling `predict` then `explain`, and **its score matched run 3's direct tool call in every digit, on a different cluster**. **Every number in its answer came from a tool; one claim did not**: it said the model was *trained on* events v92, where 92 is the version the tools read and the champion trained on v91 -- a correct number attached to a false relationship, recorded for Phase 10's verifier rather than fixed. **Seven defects no local suite could reach**, four fixed and three recorded: a cluster library set missing `lightgbm`, `anyio.run` inside the job's own running loop, a 403 that left the audit log empty, `READY` that is not callable, `gpt-oss-120b` through `OpenAIChatModel`, an empty `system.serving.endpoint_usage`, and a UC volume that refuses appends. **The eighth was in the answer's own evidence.** The model's one audit record read **212.4 s**: two tool calls (143.6 s and 61.4 s) plus about 7.4 s of model time. It also sat after the tools the model chose and named only the last model to answer, so a mid-run fallback would have been invisible -- and the exit-gate row *every model choice appears in the audit log* could not honestly be ticked. `AuditedModel` now records each request as it happens, with its own latency, model name and failure; `build_agent` applies it, so no agent is built unaudited; and a streamed request is refused rather than passed through with no record. Verified locally, not live: no sixth run was bought to watch it. `answer` no longer takes the audit log, since the model it drives already writes to it. **The live transcript is committed** (`tests/fixtures/transcripts/`) and replays through the agent for free. **Not done: the cost.** The gateway keeps no token count (the transcript does: 5,865 input and 234 output tokens), and `endpoint_usage` has no rows, so the plan's reconciliation cannot run here; `system.billing.usage` is read on or after 2026-09-11 and no cost is quoted before it. `docs/findings/2026-09-10-agent-layer-window.md`. Not pushed: the full `make check` has not run since this change. |
| 2026-09-10 | Phase 10 Task 1 -- the provenance schema says what it is versions *of* | TDD with **RED watched** (`FeatureProvenance(read_delta_versions=...)` raised `Field required: delta_versions` before the rename); `make check-fast` -- lint, `mypy --strict`, **412 passed**; every agent test module, Spark included, green (36 then 24 across two runs) | **The cause behind Phase 9's one false claim, fixed.** `ModelProvenance.delta_versions` and `FeatureProvenance.delta_versions` carried the Delta versions the tools *read*; the model saw `{"model_version": "2", "delta_versions": {...}}` and wrote "trained on Delta versions 92" when the champion trained on v91. Both fields are now `read_delta_versions` -- across `schemas.py`, three construction sites in `tools.py`, ~15 test assertions, and the two `provenance` blocks of the committed live transcript. A pure rename: no value or behaviour changed, and the model's answer text in the fixture -- the false claim included -- is untouched. `VersionsResult.training_data_delta_versions` and the `_delta_versions_from_tag` helper are deliberately left, because they already name training data; the word-boundary rename cannot reach them. The negative test in `test_agent_schemas.py` rejects the pre-rename key on **both** models, so a partial rename -- schema changed, a construction site missed -- fails to build rather than serving the old shape. The verifier is still the enforcement (Tasks 3-4); this removes the reading that invited the claim. `docs/findings/2026-09-10-agent-layer-window.md` records the fixture migration and that the original matched the window evidence at sha256 `2ab0aa9cab7b`. Not pushed: the full `make check` has not run since this change. |
| 2026-09-10 | Phase 10 Task 2 -- the grounding trace, defined before the checks that fill it | TDD with **RED watched** (`ModuleNotFoundError`, no `almanac.agent.grounding`); `make check-fast` -- lint, `mypy --strict`, **416 passed** | **The verifier's output is a decision with its reasons attached, not a score.** `GroundingTrace` is one record per agent run: a `NumberCheck` per numeric literal (its source -- exact, rounded, truncated, or unmatched -- and the tool field it traced to), a `ClaimCheck` per typed claim (the field its number had to trace to, and whether it did), a `DirectionCheck` per directional statement, a `verdict`, and a `failures` list that is empty exactly when the verdict is `grounded`. `Strict` like every schema here -- an unknown field fails to build -- and it round-trips through JSON because Task 9 writes it beside the transcript. Tasks 3, 4 and 7 fill it; this task is only its shape, so each check appends to a structure that already exists rather than one retrofitted at the end. |
| 2026-09-10 | Phase 10 Task 3 -- numeric grounding, with a rounding rule at its boundary | TDD, table-driven over the rule; `make check-fast` -- lint, `mypy --strict`, **429 passed** | **Every numeric literal in an answer is a tool-return value, or the run is ungrounded -- and the rule for "is" is stated so it can be tested.** `literals()` pulls the numbers a reader would say the answer states, after removing quoted spans (a feature name may carry a digit) and echoed timestamps (`as_of` is neither computed nor rounded). `numeric_leaves()` walks every tool return, reading a numeric string (`model_version` is `"2"`) as a number because the model does. A literal written to `d` decimals is grounded by a value when they are equal, when the value rounds to it at `d`, or when the value truncated at `d` is it -- **and a rounding match needs the literal to carry two significant digits**, so `0.004` for a returned `0.0038` is unmatched, not grounded, and an integer never rounds. The window's `0.003826` grounds `predict`'s `0.0038260014552166737` as **rounded**; a fabricated `0.5` is `unmatched` and fails the run. The live transcript grounds every number it states, because every number in it did come from a tool -- the false *claim* in it is Task 4's, not this one's. |
| 2026-09-10 | Phase 10 Task 4 -- relationship grounding: the claim must trace to the *right* field | TDD (`check_claims` cases against no implementation), the live transcript as the known-bad fixture; `make check-fast` -- lint, `mypy --strict`, **436 passed** | **The check design S6 did not have, and the window is the reason it needs one.** Every number in the window's false answer was grounded by Task 3 -- the defect was the verb. "Trained on version 92" is a claim of *training* provenance and must trace to `training_data_delta_versions`; 92 traced to `read_delta_versions`, the versions the tools read. `_CLAIM_RULES` is a **closed set of five patterns** (training / read provenance, model version, score, baseline), each mapped to the field its type requires. A claim whose number does not trace to that field -- or whose field no tool in the run returned -- is a failure that makes the run ungrounded, with a detail line naming where the number *did* trace, or which tool was never called (`versions`). An unrecognized claim shape is **not** failed; its numbers still go through Task 3, and the trace records that no rule applied. The live transcript now verifies **ungrounded**: the training claim's `92` has no `training_data_delta_versions` to trace to, because the agent never called `versions` -- exactly the gap. Its `score` and `model version` claims trace fine. One Task 3 test moved from asserting `verify()`'s verdict to asserting `check_numbers` directly, because Task 4 is what turns this fixture's verdict ungrounded. |
| 2026-09-10 | Phase 10 Task 5 -- retry once, then abstain | TDD with **RED watched** (`ImportError: cannot import name 'Ungrounded'`); `make check-fast` -- lint, `mypy --strict` (181 files), **440 passed** | **A failed verification is not a crash and not a silent pass -- it is one more turn, then a typed refusal.** `answer_grounded()` runs the bounded loop, `verify()`s the answer, and on a failure hands the verifier's own failed rows back to the model with `_RETRY_PROMPT` and exactly one more turn. If the retry grounds, its answer is returned as `Answered`; if it does not, the run ends `Ungrounded`, carrying the last answer, the failing `GroundingTrace`, and `retries=1` -- never a number it could not ground. `Ungrounded` joins the `Answered | Incomplete` outcome union, the same structured-refusal shape as `predict`'s drift refusal. **The retry spends a budgeted turn, not a free one**: it re-enters `agent.run` with the first run's request count seeded into `RunUsage` against the same `turn_limit`, so `turn_limit=2` -- one tool call and one reply -- leaves the retry no turn and the run abstains without asking the model again (the two-response test script proves the third call never happens). `answer()` is unchanged; `window.py` still calls it, and wiring `answer_grounded` into the live run is Task 9's if it lands there. |
| 2026-09-10 | Phase 10 Task 6 -- the golden set | TDD; `scripts/build_golden_fixtures.py` builds six committed fixtures, one parametrized test per entry; `make check-fast` -- lint, `mypy --strict` (182 files), **450 passed** | **Questions with the bar they should clear, committed as JSON, replayed offline.** `tests/fixtures/golden/*.json` -- each a description, the tools a correct run must call, the outcome expected, and an inline transcript (or a pointer to the committed window one). The bar has two parts: the answer verifies, **and** the run called the tools its question needs. Five synthetic known-good fixtures (predict alone; predict + explain; versions + predict + explain, where "trained on v91" traces distinct from the v92 read; a reduced-era refusal, which verifies clean because it states no number) and one synthetic known-bad -- `provenance-answer-skips-versions`, where every number is real and traces, but the question asks what the champion trained on and `versions` was never called: a golden failure on the tool sequence, not on any number, exactly the exit-gate case. The committed 2026-09-10 window transcript is the sixth entry, the real known-bad. **The golden set caught a `_CLAIM_RULES` over-match**: the model-version pattern read "the champion was trained on ... version 91" as a *model-version* claim (91 vs. the live model version 2) as well as a training one, because it allowed any span between "champion" and "version N". Tightened with a `(?!\btrain)` guard between the anchor and `version`, still five patterns, with a regression test. Fixtures are deterministic (fixed timestamps and ids) so a rebuild is a no-op unless content changed. |
| 2026-09-10 | Phase 10 Task 7 -- directional assertions | TDD with **RED watched** (`ImportError: cannot import name 'check_directions'`); flipped fixture derived from the window transcript; `make check-fast` -- lint, `mypy --strict` (183 files), **457 passed** | **A claim that a factor raises risk must not cite a contribution that lowers it.** `check_directions()` reads each `explain` contribution's direction (from its sign when the field is absent), finds the sentence in the answer that names the feature, and takes the sentence's directional language -- one of a raises-risk set (`increase`, `raise`, `elevate`, `drive up`, ...) or a lowers-risk set (`decrease`, `lower`, `reduce`, `mitigate`, `drive down`, ...). A sentence with both, or neither, is ambiguous and produces no check. A `DirectionCheck` whose `claimed` disagrees with the contribution's `actual` sign makes the run ungrounded, wired into `verify()` alongside the number and claim failures. The window's own answer is **correct** here -- its top three drivers "decrease the risk of breach" and their contributions are all negative -- so the first failing fixture is `2026-09-10-live-predict-explain-directions-flipped.json`, one phrase inverted (`decrease` -> `increase`), same numbers, asserted ungrounded on all three drivers. `scripts/build_golden_fixtures.py` derives it from the real transcript and refuses if the anchor phrase moves. |
| 2026-09-10 | Phase 10 Task 8 -- mutation-test the verifier | `test_agent_grounding_mutations.py`, one mutation per check, each with a probe that must flip; `make check-fast` -- lint, `mypy --strict`, **463 passed** | **A verifier nobody tried to break is a control of unknown scope -- the `pseudonymity.py` failure again (design S6, "not optional").** Six mutations, each `monkeypatch`ed into `grounding` (or `bounded_agent`) with a probe call whose result must change: scientific notation dropped from `_LITERAL` (a real `1.5e-3` stops grounding `0.0015`); the rounding rule widened to a band via `_MIN_SIGNIFICANT_DIGITS_TO_ROUND = 0` (`0.004` now grounds `0.0038`); the `training_provenance` rule deleted from `_CLAIM_RULES` (the window transcript goes green); a required field no tool returned counted as traced (same, by the other path); the directional sign comparison inverted (a correct answer goes red); and `verify()` stubbed to always return `grounded`, so `answer_grounded` returns `Answered` where it returned `Ungrounded` -- the downgrade Task 5's abstain exists to prevent. Each probe is asserted correct against the real verifier first, then asserted to flip under the mutation; a mutation that flips nothing fails with "nothing caught", which is the missing-test signal. |
| 2026-09-10 | Phase 10 Task 9 -- the CI gate, and the trace on disk (last build task) | `test_agent_grounding_trace_on_disk.py`, a golden meta-test naming the gate; `make check-fast` -- lint, `mypy --strict` (185 files), **467 passed** | **The gate is the offline pytest step CI already runs, not a new job.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `check` job runs `pytest -m "not network"` unfiltered, which already includes Tasks 6-8's golden and mutation tests -- a committed hallucination (the flipped or window-training fixture) fails that step today, no endpoint call, no separate job, exactly design S6's gate. **The trace on disk**: `grounding.write_trace(transcript, path)` runs `verify` and writes the `GroundingTrace` as `<stem>.grounding.json`; `almanac/agent/window.py`'s `_run_agent` calls it beside `save_transcript` on every non-`Incomplete` outcome, and now calls `answer_grounded` rather than bare `answer` -- the live window, when it runs again, self-verifies rather than only recording. The 2026-09-10 window transcript and its directions-flipped sibling both get a committed trace retroactively (`scripts/build_golden_fixtures.py`'s new `_write_grounding_traces` step); a test asserts each stays byte-identical to what `verify()` says today, so a drifted trace fails the build rather than going stale silently. Nine of nine build tasks done; Task 10 (the `v1.1.0` close-out) runs after this phase's PR merges. |

README's architecture diagram gained a `Grounding verifier` node under the agent subgraph; a new "Why this project" section carries the first-time-reader narrative (problem/motivation, what it's for, key takeaways and roadmap); "What has been measured" gained a Phase 10 paragraph.
